        7.     Closing Date. Purchaser shall tender the balance of the Purchase Price and acquire
the Property from Seller (the “Closing”) on the date (the “Closing Date”) which is the later of (i)
sixty (60) days after the Effective Date, or, (ii) ten (10) days after all of the conditions precedent
to Closing have been fulfilled, subject to adjournment as may be specifically set forth herein and
in the OTA. {

       8.      Title

             a.      Title to the Real Property conveyed hereby at Closing shall be insurable as
good and marketable at market rates with a title company authorized to do business in the State of
Rhode Island. The Property shall be conveyed to Purchaser free and clear of all liens,

                                                  5

===== PAGE 87 =====
encumbrances, easements, reservations, limitations, covenants, agreements, conditions and
restrictions, other than the following (the “Permitted Exceptions”): (i) real estate taxes and
assessments assessed or imposed by a public body upon the Property or the operation thereof which
are not due and payable or are being contested in good faith by appropriate proceedings on the
Closing Date, provided that the payment of the taxes is not required to bring such contest and
Seller puts into escrow the amount required by the Title Company to omit any exception for taxes
on the title policy, which relate to the period from and after the Closing Date, (ii) the requirements
of any federal, state or municipal laws, ordinances, rules and regulations; and (iii) easements,
covenants, encroachments, agreements, reservations, encumbrances, restrictions, rights-of-way
and any other non-monetary title defects and zoning, building and other similar restrictions which
are of record as of the Effective Date; provided, however, that none of the foregoing described in
this subsection (iii) are currently violated or will individually or in the aggregate materially
adversely impair the continued use, access to, value or marketability of the Property or the
operation of the Facility as presently conducted.

                b.      No later than ten (10) days after the Effective Date, Purchaser shall, at
Purchaser’s sole cost and expense, order a title commitment for the Property (the “Title
Commitment”) from a title company authorized to do business in the State of Rhode Island (the
“Title Company”) which shall be selected by Purchaser. No later than ten (10) days after the
Effective Date, Purchaser shall, at Purchaser’s sole cost and expense, order an ALTA survey from
a licensed surveyor in the State of Rhode Island (the “Survey”). If the Title Commitment lists
exceptions to coverage, other than Permitted Exceptions, which are not acceptable to Purchaser,
or the Survey shows any defects to title which are not Permitted Exceptions (collectively, the “Title
Objections”), Purchaser shall notify Seller of such Title Objections within ten (10) days after
receipt of the Title Commitment and the Survey (the “Purchaser’s Title Objection Notice”). In
the event Purchaser fails to deliver to Seller the Purchaser’s Title Objection Notice in the time
period set forth above, Purchaser shall be deemed to have accepted the Title Commitment and
Survey “as is” and waived any objections thereto. Seller shall notify Purchaser of its intent to
attempt to cure or not to cure the Title Objections (the “Seller’s Title Notice”) within ten (10)
business days after receipt of the Purchaser’s Title Objection Notice (the “Seller’s Title Notice
Date”). In the event that Seller does not deliver to Purchaser the Seller’s Title Notice by the Seller’s
Title Notice Date, Seller shall be deemed to have elected not to cure any Title Objections. In the
event Seller elects to attempt to cure any of the Title Objections, Seller shall, using commercially
reasonable efforts (i) attempt to cause such Title Objections to be cured within forty-five (45) days
of the Seller’s Title Notice, or (ii) assist Purchaser in obtaining affirmative title insurance coverage
from the Title Company over same to the commercially reasonable satisfaction of Purchaser;
provided, however, that Seller may request an extension of such date and Purchaser shall grant
such extension so long as any delay is based upon circumstances beyond the reasonable control of
Seller and such extension does not exceed thirty (30) additional days(such extended date the
“Seller’s Title Cure Date”). Seller shall be responsible for the payment of any recording costs
associated with the cure of the Title Objections. If Seller shall not have cured all of the Title
Objections which it has elected to cure to Purchaser’s commercially reasonable satisfaction by the
Seller’s Title Cure Date then Purchaser shall have the right to proceed to Closing without
abatement of the Purchase Price or terminate this Agreement, whereupon the Deposit shall be
returned to the Purchaser, this Agreement shall be null and void and of no further force and effect
other than those items specifically agreed upon to survive termination or earlier expiration of this
Agreement. If Seller’s Title Notice notifies Purchaser of Seller’s intent not to remove all of the
Title Objections or if Seller is deemed to have elected not to cure all of the Title Objections,

                                                   6

===== PAGE 88 =====
Purchaser, at its option, may on written notice (“Purchaser’s Title Reply Notice”) to Seller within
ten (10) days after receipt of Seller’s Title Notice either (i) terminate this Agreement, the Deposit
shall be returned to the Purchaser and neither party shall have any further liability to the other
hereunder; or (ii) accept title pursuant hereto without any adjustment in the Purchase Price,
provided, however, that Seller shall remain obligated to remove any Title Objections which it has
agreed to remove in accordance with this Section.

                c.     Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, Seller shall discharge at
Closing all monetary liens and encumbrances that affect any portion of the Property, including,
but not limited to, mortgages, liens and judgments or, if acceptable to the Title Company, Seller
may escrow the funds necessary to remove or bond over such lien so that the Title Company shall
provide title insurance without excepting for such lien.

               d.       In the event that after the Effective Date, any additional title search or any
survey shall disclose any additional Title Objections which become of record subsequent to the
effective and/or certification date of the initial Title Commitment or Survey, the parties shall have
the same rights, remedies and obligations as provided for in subsections (b) and (c) of this Section
8 except that Purchaser and Seller shall have ten (10) days to send the Purchaser’s Title Objection
Notice and Seller’s Title Notice, as applicable.

       9.     Seller’s Representations. Real Property Seller and Operator, jointly and severally,
hereby represent, warrant and covenant that as of the Effective Date and as of the Closing Date:

                a.     Real Property Seller is a non-profit corporation duly organized and validly
existing and in good standing under the laws of the State of Rhode Island with full corporate power
and authority to enter into and, subject to the conditions herein contained, to perform the terms
and conditions of this Agreement. Seller has obtained the approval of the General Association of
the Pious Society of Saint Charles for this transaction as well as for the conveyance of the adjacent
property owned by the Seller in accordance with a Purchase Option to be executed on the same
date hereof, including but not limited to, the Seller’s obligation to sell the adjacent property to a
third party subject to the rights of the Optionee thereunder. No approval from the Attorney
General, Court or similar authority is required for the non-profit Seller to convey its assets to a for
profit entity. Old Operator is a corporation duly organized and validly existing and in good
standing under the laws of the State of Rhode Island with full corporate power and authority to
enter into and, subject to the conditions herein contained, to perform the terms and conditions of
this Agreement. This Agreement constitutes a legal, valid and binding obligation of each Seller,
enforceable in accordance with its terms, subject to applicable bankruptcy, insolvency,
reorganization, moratorium or similar laws affecting the rights of creditors generally.

                b.     To Seller’s knowledge (i) Real Property Seller owns good and marketable
title to the Real Property, free and clear of all liens and encumbrances, except for the Permitted
Exceptions, and (ii) Old Operator owns good and marketable title to the Assets, except the Real
Property, free and clear of all liens and encumbrances, except for any Permitted Exceptions and
any leased Assets which are set forth in Schedule 8(b) of the OTA. The Old Operator as of the
date of this Agreement is the lessee operator of the Facility and together with Real Property Seller,
other than the Excluded Assets, owns all of the Assets, except to the extent transferred pursuant to
the OTA and except any leased Assets which are set forth in Schedule 8(b) of the OTA. Any lease
between Real Property Seller and the New Operator or any of their affiliates will be terminated as
of the Closing.

(iii) suffered the appointment of a receiver to take possession of all or substantially all of its assets,
(iv) suffered the attachment or judicial seizure of all, or substantially all, of its assets, (v) admitted
in writing its inability to pay its debts as they come due, or (vi) made an offer of settlement,
extension or composition of its creditors generally.

                c.     To Purchaser’s knowledge, there is no condition, past or current conduct,
actions or omissions, or assertions or investigations relating thereto, of or related to the Purchaser
or any of its affiliates (as such term is broadly defined under the Federal Securities Laws
(“Affiliate”)) why the Closing Approvals will not be granted.

               d.     Purchaser has sufficient cash on hand or other sources of immediately
available funds to enable it to make payment of the Purchase Price and consummate the
transactions contemplated by this Agreement.

                e.      Purchaser has conducted its own independent investigation, review and
analysis of the Business and the Assets, and acknowledges that it has been provided adequate
access to the personnel, properties, assets, premises, books and records, and other documents and
data of Seller for such purpose. Purchaser acknowledges and agrees that: (a) in making its decision
to enter into this Agreement and to consummate the transactions contemplated hereby, Purchaser
has relied solely upon its own investigation and the express representations, warranties covenants
and obligations of Seller set forth in this Agreement and the OTA (including related portions of
the Disclosure Schedules); and (b) neither Seller nor any other Person has made any representation
or warranty as to Seller, the Business, the Assets or this Agreement, except as expressly set forth
this Agreement and the OTA (including the related portions of the Disclosure Schedules).

               f.     All representations of the Purchaser shall survive Closing for a period of
eighteen (18) months.

       17.     Closing Prorations.

                a.      Real estate taxes shall be prorated in accordance with local custom,
including the establishment of any escrow for taxes to be paid for periods prior to the Closing Date
but the amount thereof will not be known until after the Closing Date. If any additional or
supplemental taxes are assessed against the Property for back assessments, corrections to previous
tax bills or other events occurring before the Closing Date, including without limitation the
imposition of any retroactive assessments relating to improvements made prior to the Closing
Date, the parties shall make any necessary adjustments after the Closing. This Section is to survive
Closing for a period of sixty (60) days.

                b.     At the Closing, Purchaser and Seller shall adjust for rents, utilities and other
charges collected, assessments, water and sewer service as of the Closing Date, Service Contracts
to the extent assumed by the Purchaser and other customary prorations. The parties shall prorate
any signing or other bonus received by the Seller for any of the Service Contracts or other
agreements being assumed by the Purchaser. Seller shall obtain final meter readings immediately
prior to Closing. In the event final meter readings are not available, readings shall be estimated
based on the most recent reading and the parties shall agree to reconcile any discrepancies in the
actual amount upon receipt of final readings, which obligation shall survive closing and delivery
of the deed. Seller shall provide the Title Company with any escrows required for any lienable
utilities.

                c.      All installment payments of special assessments, if any, which are due and
payable at any time prior to the Closing Date shall be paid by Seller on or prior to the Closing
Date. All installment payments of special assessments, if any, which are due and payable at any
time on or after the Closing Date shall be paid by Purchaser. Notwithstanding the above, in the
event any special assessments are due and payable after the Closing Date and such assessments
are specifically identified as applying to matters occurring prior to the Closing Date, the Seller
shall be responsible for such payment but only to the extent that such assessments relate to periods
of time prior to the Closing Date. In addition, in the event any special assessments are due and
payable on or prior to the Closing Date and such assessments are specifically identified as applying
to matters occurring after the Closing Date, the Purchaser shall be responsible for such payment.

                 d.      The Closing shall become effective and Purchaser shall take over the
operations of the Facility as of 12:01 AM on the Closing Date (the “Effective Time”) and, except
as otherwise set forth in this Agreement, Seller shall be entitled to all benefits and be responsible
for all obligations of the Facility related to the period prior to the Effective Time and the Purchaser
shall be entitled to the benefits and be responsible for the liabilities related to the period from and
after the Effective Time. All prorations shall be made as of the Closing Date based upon the most
current information then available. The parties agree that except as provided for herein, to the
extent final prorations for anything other than real estate taxes cannot be determined because of
lack of data, information or readings accurate as of the Closing Date, the parties shall adjust any
prorations made on the Closing Statement based on the best information available and shall re-
prorate such amounts when the information becomes available but no later than sixty (60) days
after the Closing. Both parties shall respond promptly to requests for data, information or readings

                                                  15

===== PAGE 97 =====
from the other and execute any additional addenda to the Closing Statement as may be reasonably
necessary to reflect any adjustments. This Paragraph shall survive Closing.

       19.     Indemnity.

                 a.      Beginning from the Closing Date and continuing for eighteen (18) months
thereafter, Seller jointly and severally hereby indemnifies and agrees to defend and hold Purchaser
and its successors, assigns, affiliates, managers, members, agents, servants, employees, and any
tenant operator of the Facility, harmless from and against any and all claims, demands, obligations,
losses, liabilities, damages, recoveries and deficiencies (including interest, penalties and
reasonable attorneys’ fees, costs and expenses) (collectively the “Losses”) which any of them may
suffer as a result of any matter which relates to the period prior to Closing or to Seller’s obligations
which survive Closing, arising out of the following (“Indemnification Events”): (i) the breach
of any of the representations or warranties of Seller herein; (ii) any default by Seller in the
performance of any of its commitments, covenants or obligations under this Agreement (iii) all
liabilities not assumed by Purchaser including all Excluded Liabilities, (iv) all indemnification
obligations of the Old Operator pursuant to the OTA, or (v) any matter related to Excluded Assets.

                b.       Beginning from the Closing Date and continuing for eighteen (18) months
thereafter, Purchaser shall defend and indemnify Seller, and its successors, assigns, affiliates,
managers, partners, agents, servants, employees (the “Seller Indemnitees”), harmless from and
against any and all claims, demands, obligations, losses, liabilities, damages, recoveries and
deficiencies (including interest, penalties and reasonable attorneys’ fees, costs and expenses)
which any of them may suffer as a result of any of the following events that Seller Indemnitees
incur as a result of, or with respect to: (i) the breach of any of the representations or warranties of
Purchaser herein; (ii) any default by Purchaser in the performance of any of its commitments,
covenants or obligations under this Agreement, (iii) the Assumed Liabilities, any matter related to
the Assumed Liabilities, or (iv) any obligations of New Operator as set forth in Section 3(d).

                c.       If any party (the “Indemnified Party”) receives notice of the assertion or
commencement of any action, suit, claim or other legal proceeding made or brought by any Person
who is not a party to this Agreement or an Affiliate of a party to this Agreement or a Representative
of the foregoing (a “Third-Party Claim”) against such Indemnified Party with respect to which
the Indemnifying Party is obligated to provide indemnification under this Agreement, the
Indemnified Party shall give the Indemnifying Party prompt written notice thereof. The failure to
give such prompt written notice shall not, however, relieve the Indemnifying Party of its
indemnification obligations, except and only to the extent that the Indemnifying Party forfeits
rights or defenses by reason of such failure. Such notice by the Indemnified Party shall describe
the Third-Party Claim in reasonable detail, shall include copies of all material written evidence
thereof and shall indicate the estimated amount, if reasonably practicable, of the Loss that has been
or may be sustained by the Indemnified Party. The Indemnifying Party shall have the right to
participate in, or by giving written notice to the Indemnified Party, to assume the defense of any
Third-Party Claim at the Indemnifying Party's expense and by the Indemnifying Party's own
counsel, and the Indemnified Party shall cooperate in good faith in such defense. In the event that
the Indemnifying Party assumes the defense of any Third-Party Claim, subject to Section 19(d), it

                                                  16

===== PAGE 98 =====
shall have the right to take such action as it deems necessary to avoid, dispute, defend, appeal or
make counterclaims pertaining to any such Third-Party Claim in the name and on behalf of the
Indemnified Party. The Indemnified Party shall have the right, at its own cost and expense, to
participate in the defense of any Third-Party Claim with counsel selected by it subject to the
Indemnifying Party's right to control the defense thereof. If the Indemnifying Party elects not to
compromise or defend such Third-Party Claim or fails to promptly notify the Indemnified Party in
writing of its election to defend as provided in this Agreement, the Indemnified Party may, subject
to Section 19(d), pay, compromise, defend such Third-Party Claim and seek indemnification for
any and all Losses based upon, arising from or relating to such Third-Party Claim. Seller and
Purchaser shall cooperate with each other in all reasonable respects in connection with the defense
of any Third-Party Claim, including making available records relating to such Third-Party Claim
and furnishing, without expense (other than reimbursement of actual out-of-pocket expenses) to
the defending party, management employees of the non-defending party as may be reasonably
necessary for the preparation of the defense of such Third-Party Claim.

                 d.      Notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, the Indemnifying
Party shall not enter into settlement of any Third-Party Claim without the prior written consent of
the Indemnified Party (which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed), except as
provided in this Section 19(d). If a firm offer is made to settle a Third-Party Claim without leading
to liability or the creation of a financial or other obligation on the part of the Indemnified Party
and provides, in customary form, for the unconditional release of each Indemnified Party from all
liabilities and obligations in connection with such Third-Party Claim and the Indemnifying Party
desires to accept and agree to such offer, the Indemnifying Party shall give written notice to that
effect to the Indemnified Party. If the Indemnified Party fails to consent to such firm offer within
ten days after its receipt of such notice, the Indemnified Party may continue to contest or defend
such Third-Party Claim and in such event, the maximum liability of the Indemnifying Party as to
such Third-Party Claim shall not exceed the amount of such settlement offer. If the Indemnified
Party fails to consent to such firm offer and also fails to assume defense of such Third-Party Claim,
the Indemnifying Party may settle the Third-Party Claim upon the terms set forth in such firm offer
to settle such Third-Party Claim. If the Indemnified Party has assumed the defense pursuant to
Section 19(c), it shall not agree to any settlement without the written consent of the Indemnifying
Party (which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed).

                 e.     Any claim by an Indemnified Party on account of a Loss which does not
result from a Third-Party Claim (a “Direct Claim”) shall be asserted by the Indemnified Party
giving the Indemnifying Party prompt written notice thereof. The failure to give such prompt
written notice shall not, however, relieve the Indemnifying Party of its indemnification obligations,
except and only to the extent that the Indemnifying Party forfeits rights or defenses by reason of
such failure. Such notice by the Indemnified Party shall describe the Direct Claim in reasonable
detail, shall include copies of all material written evidence thereof and shall indicate the estimated
amount, if reasonably practicable, of the Loss that has been or may be sustained by the Indemnified
Party. The Indemnifying Party shall have ten (10) days after its receipt of such notice to respond
in writing to such Direct Claim. During such ten (10) day period, the Indemnified Party shall allow
the Indemnifying Party and its professional advisors to investigate the matter or circumstance
alleged to give rise to the Direct Claim, and whether and to what extent any amount is payable in
respect of the Direct Claim and the Indemnified Party shall assist the Indemnifying Party's
investigation by giving such information and assistance (including access to the Indemnified
Party's premises and personnel and the right to examine and copy any accounts, documents or


                                                 17

===== PAGE 99 =====
records) as the Indemnifying Party or any of its professional advisors may reasonably request. If
the Indemnifying Party does not so respond within such thirty (30) day period, the Indemnifying
Party shall be deemed to have rejected such claim, in which case the Indemnified Party shall be
free to pursue such remedies as may be available to the Indemnified Party on the terms and subject
to the provisions of this Agreement.

                f.      In order to secure the indemnities provided by Seller and other obligations
of Seller provided for herein, at Closing, Seller and Purchaser agree to enter into an Indemnity
Escrow Agreement in substantially the form of Exhibit D (the “Indemnity Escrow Agreement”)
under which an amount equal to Two Hundred Thousand ($200,000) Dollars shall be held in
escrow (the “Indemnity Escrow Fund”) with the escrow Agent to be used to cover Seller’s
indemnity and other obligations hereunder. The Indemnity Escrow Agreement shall provide that
the Indemnity Escrow Fund shall be held by the Escrow Agent in an interest-bearing account and
all earnings shall accrue to the escrow account and be disbursed with the escrow funds. The
Indemnity Escrow Agreement shall set out a procedure for claims to be made by Purchaser and for
all payments from the Indemnity Escrow Fund to be paid based upon joint escrow instructions
signed by Seller and Purchaser hereunder. Upon the date which is eighteen (18) months following
the Closing Date, Seller shall be entitled to receive the remaining balance of the Indemnity Escrow
Fund, less the amount of any prior payment and less the amount of any pending claims. Purchaser
covenants and agrees that it shall first seek recovery against the Indemnity Escrow but that nothing
contained in this Section shall be deemed to limit Seller’s indemnification obligation to the amount
of the Indemnification Escrow.

              g.      The obligations of Seller and Purchaser pursuant to this Section shall
survive Closing until such time as neither party have any obligations to each other, provided,
however, that in any event, to the extent that there are Indemnification Events for which an
indemnity has been claimed, then this indemnity shall survive until the resolution of such
Indemnification Events.

                h.      Notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, to the extent that
(i) any Third Party Claim is collected from the Purchaser, (ii) a non-appealable judgment is entered
against the Seller which judgment has a material effect on the Purchaser, or (iii) if the failure to
pay any Third Party Claim will material and adversely affect the Purchaser’s operations and
Purchaser has made such payment (subject to the dispute resolution provisions set forth in this
Section), then in each such event, Seller shall reimburse the Purchaser for such amount within ten
(10) days of notice thereof and Seller shall have the right to appeal such claim and take any other
legal action against the third party making the Third Party Claim in accordance with the provisions
of this Section 20. To the extent that Seller fails to pay Purchaser for any matter for which Seller
is required to indemnify Purchaser within thirty (30) days of notice thereof, such amount shall
accrue interest at seven and one-half (7.5%) percent per annum. In the event that the parties cannot
agree whether or not the non-payment of any Third Party Claim will materially and adversely
affect the Purchaser’s operations, either party shall submit such matter to arbitration in accordance
with the requirements of the American Arbitration Association and the decision of such arbitrator
shall be final.

        20.     Certain Limitations of Indemnification. The party making a claim under Section
19 is referred to as the “Indemnified Party”, and the party against whom such claims are asserted
under Section 19 is referred to as the “Indemnifying Party”. The indemnification provided for in
Section 19(a) and 19 (b) shall be subject to the following limitations:

                                                 18

===== PAGE 100 =====
                 a.      With respect to any claim as to which the Indemnified Party may be entitled
to indemnification under Section 19(a)(i) or 19(b)(i), as the case may be, the Indemnifying Party
shall not be liable for any individual or series of related Losses which do not exceed Fifty Thousand
Dollars ($50,000) (which Losses shall be counted toward the Deductible at which point the
Indemnifying Party shall be liable from dollar one.

               b.      The aggregate amount of all Losses for which an Indemnifying Party shall
be liable pursuant to Section 19(a)(i) or 19(b)(i), as the case may be, shall not exceed Four Million
Five Hundred Thousand Dollars ($4,500,000).

                c.      Payments by an Indemnifying Party pursuant to Section 19(a)(i) or 19(b)(i)
in respect of any Loss shall be limited to the amount of any liability or damage that remains after
deducting therefrom any insurance proceeds and any indemnity, contribution or other similar
payment received or reasonably expected to be received by the Indemnified Party in respect of any
such claim. To the extent any claim may be covered by Seller’s insurance and to the extent that
any delay in seeking insurance funds will not prejudice any claims, Purchaser agrees for Seller to
use its commercially reasonable efforts to recover under insurance policies or indemnity,
contribution or other similar agreements for any Losses which shall be provided to Purchaser prior
to Purchaser seeking indemnification under this Agreement.

               d.     Payments by an Indemnifying Party pursuant to Section 19(a)(i) or 19(b)(i)
in respect of any Loss shall be reduced by an amount equal to any Tax benefit realized or
reasonably expected to be realized as a result of such Loss by the Indemnified Party.

               e.      In no event shall any Indemnifying Party be liable to any Indemnified Party
for any punitive, incidental, consequential, special or indirect damages, including loss of future
revenue or income, loss of business reputation or opportunity relating to the breach or alleged
breach of this Agreement, or diminution of value or any damages based on any type of multiple.

               f.    Each Indemnified Party shall take, and cause its Affiliates to take, all
reasonable steps to mitigate any Loss upon becoming aware of any event or circumstance that
would be reasonably expected to, or does, give rise thereto, including incurring costs only to the
minimum extent necessary to remedy the breach that gives rise to such Loss.

               g.       Seller shall not be liable under Section 19(a) for any Losses based upon or
arising out of any inaccuracy in or breach of any of the representations or warranties of Seller
contained in this Agreement if Buyer had actual knowledge of such inaccuracy or breach prior to
the Closing, except for Third Party Claims as set forth in Section 11(h). For the avoidance of
doubt, Seller shall be responsible to reimburse the Purchaser for third party claims, regardless of
whether Purchaser had knowledge of such claim prior to Closing.

                h.     All indemnification payments made under this Agreement shall be treated
by the parties as an adjustment to the Purchase Price for tax purposes, unless otherwise required
by applicable law.

       21.     Casualty Loss.

                a.     The risk of loss or damage to the Property caused by fire or other casualty
prior to the Closing shall be borne by Seller. If prior to Closing, the Property, or any part thereof,

                                                 19

===== PAGE 101 =====
is damaged as the result of fire or other casualty such that the cost of repair or replacement, is
reasonably likely to exceed Five Hundred Thousand Dollars ($500,000), or if a material part of
the Property is taken through a condemnation action or is conveyed in connection with any eminent
domain action, Seller shall notify Purchaser of such damage, destruction or taking, (“Seller’s
Casualty Notice”) within five (5) days after such casualty, which notice shall specify all relevant
insurance information, and Purchaser shall, within five (5) days after receiving Seller’s Casualty
Notice, notify Seller (“Purchaser’s Casualty Notice”) of Purchaser’s election to either (i)
terminate this Agreement, in which event the Deposit shall be returned to Purchaser and neither
party shall have any further liability to the other, or (ii) accept title to the Property without any
abatement of the Purchase Price whatsoever, in which event at the Closing all of the insurance or
condemnation proceeds or right to such proceeds shall be assigned by Seller to Purchaser and
Seller shall credit Purchaser for the amount of any deductible. In the event that Purchaser elects
to proceed to Closing, Seller shall not settle any claim or use any of the funds without the consent
of the Purchaser. In the event that Purchaser fails to timely send the Purchaser’s Casualty Notice,
Purchaser shall be deemed to have elected to terminate this Agreement as set forth in option (i)
herein.

                 b.      If, prior to Closing, the Property, or any part thereof, is damaged but the
cost of repair is not reasonably likely to exceed Five Hundred Thousand Dollars ($500,000), or if
an immaterial part of the Property is taken through a condemnation action or is conveyed in
connection with any eminent domain action, Seller shall send Purchaser the Seller’s Casualty
Notice within five (5) days after such casualty or condemnation notice stating its intent to either
(i) repair the damage to the Property, or (ii) if the casualty is fully insured assign all of the insurance
or condemnation proceeds or right to such proceeds to Purchaser and Seller shall credit Purchaser
for the amount of any deductible and in such event, Seller shall not settle any claim or use any of
the funds without the consent of the Purchaser. If Seller elects to repair the Property per item (i)
above, in the event that Seller does not complete the repairs within sixty (60) days from the date
of Seller’s Casualty Notice, Purchaser shall have the right to either terminate this Agreement and
shall be entitled to a return of the Deposit or to proceed to Closing with a credit to the Purchase
Price in an amount necessary to restore the Property to the same condition as of the Effective Date
but not less than the amount provided by the insurance or condemnation award to repair such
damage. If Seller elects not to repair, and the insurance or condemnation proceeds are insufficient
to repair the Property to its prior condition, the Purchaser shall have the right to terminate this
Agreement, the Deposit shall be returned to Purchaser and neither party shall have any further
liability to the other. For purposes of this Section, a material part of the Property is any part of the
Property for which the cost to repair or replace would be more than Five Hundred Thousand
($500,000) Dollars or, if any access to the Property is removed or if such a taking materially affects
the owner’s ability to operate the Property in the same manner as currently being operated.

        22.    Entire Agreement. The entire agreement between Seller and Purchaser with
respect to the sale and purchase of the Property and the Facility is expressly set forth in this
Agreement and the OTA; and the parties are not bound by any agreement, understanding,
provision, condition, representation or warranty other than as are expressly set forth and stipulated
in this Agreement.

        23.     Default and Specific Enforcement.

               a.     If Purchaser defaults under this Agreement, which shall include a material
misrepresentation by the Purchaser or Purchaser’s failure to materially comply with any of its

                                                    20

===== PAGE 102 =====
obligations set forth in this Agreement, after the provision of notice by Seller and ten (10) days
opportunity to cure said default by Purchaser, the Seller may terminate this Agreement and the
Deposit shall be delivered to the Seller and the parties hereto shall thereby be relieved of all
obligations hereunder other than those specifically stated to survive termination of this Agreement.
Seller hereby waives all other rights and remedies in law or equity.

                  b.    If Seller defaults under this Agreement which shall include a material
misrepresentation by the Seller or Seller’s failure to materially comply with any of its obligations
set forth in this Agreement, after the provision of notice by Purchaser and ten (10) days opportunity
to cure said default by Seller, the Purchaser may terminate this Agreement, the Deposit shall be
returned to Purchaser and shall be entitled to reimbursement of its out of pocket costs in connection
with this transaction in the aggregate of up to $100,000 and upon payment thereof, the parties
hereto shall thereby be relieved of all obligations hereunder other than those specifically stated to
survive termination of this Agreement.

                c.     In addition to Section (a) and (b) above, Seller agrees that irreparable
damage may occur in the event that any of the provisions of this Agreement were not performed
by the Seller in accordance with their specific terms or were otherwise breached by the Seller. It
is accordingly agreed that Purchaser shall be entitled to an injunction or injunctions to prevent
breaches of this Agreement by the Seller and to enforce specifically the representations, warranties,
covenants, obligations and other terms and provisions of this Agreement exclusively in a state or
federal court located in Rhode Island. Additionally, Purchaser shall be entitled to its costs
associated with obtaining an injunction or specific performance, including reasonable attorney’s
fees.

                d.      In the event that the Closing does not take place due to the failure of any
condition precedent to Purchaser’s obligations set forth in Section 12 which is not an obligation of
Purchaser and Purchaser is not otherwise in default hereunder, Purchaser shall have the right to
terminate this Agreement and the deposit shall be returned to the Purchaser. In the event that the
Closing does not take place due to the failure of any condition precedent to Seller’s obligations set
forth in Section 13 and Seller is not otherwise in default hereunder, Seller shall have the right to
terminate this Agreement, the Deposit shall be returned to the Purchaser and the parties shall have
no further rights against each other except as may be set forth herein which survives termination
of the Agreement. The termination right set forth in this Paragraph for failure of a condition
precedent is not intended to relieve any party of its rights and remedies in the event that any party
fails to perform its obligations under this Agreement or makes any misrepresentation and in such
event, the parties shall have their respective rights and remedies set forth in subsections (a), (b)
and (c) of this Section.

       24.     Force Majeure.

             a. Neither party shall be deemed in default of this Agreement for any delay or failure
to fulfill any obligation, including but not limited to Closing, hereunder or thereunder so long as
and to the extent (i) any pandemic or epidemic causes the Facility’s operations to be adversely
affected, or (ii) to which any delay or failure in the fulfillment of such obligation is rendered
impossible or impractical due to war, acts of hostilities, acts of terrorism, declaration of a state of
emergency, pandemic, epidemic, computer virus effecting any governmental authority which is
required for the Closing, natural disaster, power or other mechanical failure, any material


                                                  21

===== PAGE 103 =====
disruption in the financial, banking or securities markets, or any condition which causes lenders to
pull lending commitments.

                 b. In the event of any such excused delay, the time for performance of such
obligations shall be extended for a period equal to the time lost by reason of the delay plus fifteen
(15) days, provided, however, that Seller shall not have the right to adjourn the Closing Date for
more than thirty (30) days. In the event that the delay, frustration, hindrance, or delay caused by
the Force Majeure set forth herein lasts for a period of more than sixty (60) days or is reasonably
likely to last for more than 60 days, Purchaser shall have the right to terminate this Agreement and
in such event the Deposit shall be returned to the Purchaser.

       25.     Parking and Easements.

             a. Private Road. The Property is accessed via a private road known as Crawford Allen
Hospital Road (the “Private Road”) which also provides access to other land being retained by
the Seller. The parties agree that the portion of the private road which is Plat 32 Lot 13 of the
North Kingstown Tax Map (the “Separate Road Area”) shall be conveyed to the Purchaser as
part of this transaction. At Closing, the parties shall execute a permanent, non-exclusive,
reciprocal access easement agreement (the “Road Access Agreement”) in the form attached
hereto as Exhibit F which shall be recorded in the local land records. The Road Access Agreement
shall provide Seller with a non-exclusive access easement for the entire Private Road, including
the portion of the Private Road located on Plat 167 Lot 3 which is being conveyed to the Purchaser,
for ingress, egress and regress to its other land being retained by Seller. Seller shall terminate all
existing easements of record which relate to access to the property which shall be superseded and
replaced by the Road Access Easement and specifically including the easement set forth in the
Deed recorded in Book 851 Page 113.

             b. Road Maintenance Agreement. The Road Access Agreement shall also provide
that Purchaser shall have the obligation to maintain the Private Road and all other property owners
serviced by the Private Road will contribute their pro rata portion of the cost of such maintenance
which agreement shall be recorded in the County land records. The Seller and Purchaser agree
that the obligation to maintain shall not include capital improvements which is defined as any
improvement which is not made in order to repair the Private Road but is performed to upgrade
the Private Road. Seller shall be pay 50% of the costs of the maintenance of the Private Road
until the sale of the remaining parcels to a third party and after such sale, the Purchaser shall be
responsible for twenty five (25%) percent of the costs and the owner of the remaining property for
seventy five (75%) percent. Any subdivision and subsequent conveyance of any land to third
parties, shall be subject to the terms of the Road Access Agreement. The parties shall execute the
Road Access Agreement on the Closing Date.

             c. Storage Areas. Seller acknowledges that vehicles and other equipment included in
this sale are stored in two garages on the Seller’s property and that another building on Seller’s
property contains storage which is used by the Facility (collectively the “Storage Areas”). Seller
agrees to continue to allow Purchaser the right to use the Storage Areas following the Closing until
such time that it cannot reasonably continue to do so and at such time Seller may terminate this
license to use any or all of the Storage Areas upon one hundred twenty (120) days written notice
to the Purchaser.



                                                 22

===== PAGE 104 =====
            d. Fiber Optic and Electricity. Seller acknowledges that the fiber optic and electrical
service to the Facility are routed on Seller’s property through the building on Seller’s property
which is adjacent to the Facility. Seller agrees to allow Purchaser to continue to use such lines and
to reasonably add services to such lines through the adjacent building until such time as it cannot
reasonably continue to do so and at such time Seller may terminate this license to use the lines
going through the adjacent building upon one hundred twenty (120) days written notice to the
Purchaser.

             e. The provisions of this Section 25 shall survive the Closing.

                                OPERATIONS TRANSFER AGREEMENT

       THIS OPERATIONS TRANSFER AGREEMENT (this “Agreement”) is made and
entered into as of this 18th
                        _____ day of January, 2021 (the “Effective Date”) by and between
SCALABRINI VILLA, INC., a Rhode Island non-profit corporation having an address of 860
North Quidnessett Road, North Kingstown, Rhode Island 02852 (the “Old Operator”), and
BAYVIEW OPERATOR LLC a Rhode Island limited liability company having an address of 1608
Route 88, Suite 200, Brick, New Jersey or another entity designated to be the new operator (the
“New Operator”).

        WHEREAS, Old Operator, currently owns and operates that certain 120 bed skilled
nursing facility commonly known as Scalabrini Villa Skilled Nursing & Rehabilitation Center (the
“Facility”) located at 860 North Quidnessett Road, North Kingstown, Rhode Island 02852 (the
“Real Property”); and,

        WHEREAS, Bayview Property LLC, a Rhode Island limited liability company having an
address of 1608 Route 88, Suite 200, Brick, NJ 08724 (“Purchaser”) agreed to acquire from The
Pious Society of the Missionaries of St. Charles Boromeo, Inc. (the “Seller”) the Real Property
and all of the furniture, fixtures and equipment and other items of personal and intangible property
and other property located at and related to the Facility (collectively the “Purchased Property”),
pursuant to and as set forth in that certain Asset Purchase Agreement by and between Seller and
Purchaser, dated as of even date herewith (the “Purchase Agreement”);

       WHEREAS, in furtherance of a desire by the parties hereto to ensure a smooth transition
of operations of the Facility, the parties hereto desire to enter into this Agreement.

        NOW THEREFORE, for good and valuable consideration and the mutual covenants and
agreements set forth herein, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged by the
parties hereto, the Old Operator and New Operator, intending to be legally bound, do hereby agree
as follows:

        1.      Property Conveyed. On the date on which the closing under the Purchase
Agreement occurs (the “Closing Date”) Old Operator agrees to transfer to New Operator and New
Operator agrees to accept from Old Operator all of Old Operator’s right, title and interest from and
after the Closing Date (as hereinafter defined) in and to the following (collectively the “Assets” or
the “Property”):

                   a. Licenses, franchises, permits, contract rights, subsidy contracts, Medicare provider
                      agreements, provider identification numbers, accreditations, third-party payor
                      agreements which are necessary for the operation of the Facility, all to the extent
                      assignable and to the extent assumed by New Operator as set forth in this
                      Agreement;

                   b. All resident contracts, Admission Agreements (as defined herein) and other
                      agreements including all guarantees thereof and all payments and deposits made by



4841-2849-7870.5

===== PAGE 112 =====
                      or on behalf of the residents of the Facility for the period on and after the Closing
                      Date (as defined herein);

                   c. Telephone numbers, web and email addresses and domain names associated with
                      the Facility, all books and records of the Old Operator in any format;

                   d. The trade names “Scalabrini” “Scalabrini Villa,” and all of the associated names
                      used by the Facility and all goodwill associated with the names “Scalabrini”
                      “Scalabrini Villa,” and associated with the Facility. As of the Closing Date, except
                      in connection with billing for pending receivables, Old Operator will cease
                      conducting any business utilizing the names “Scalabrini” “Scalabrini Villa,” or any
                      derivation of such name. All rights to the names “Scalabrini” “Scalabrini Villa,”
                      and any variation or derivation thereof, together with all of Old Operator’s rights
                      to the names used in connection with the Facility are being conveyed to New
                      Operator as part of the Property;

                   e. Service Contracts (as hereinafter defined), if any, as set forth in Section 6 and all

                      surveys, policy manuals, and forms and all of Old Operator’s artwork and other

agreed to be assumed by New Operator in this Agreement, if any (the “Assumed Liabilities”).

           5.      Access.

                a.      From the Effective Date through the Closing Date, New Operator and its
agents, engineers, surveyors, appraisers, auditors and other representatives shall have reasonable
access to the Property during normal working hours accompanied by the Facility Administrator or
such other Old Operator designee (the “Access”); provided, however, that New Operator shall
provide reasonable advanced written notice to Old Operator of the request for Access and Old
Operator shall reasonably agree to the date and time of the Access, and that he Access shall be
subject to the rights of residents. The purpose of the Access may be to inspect, examine, survey,
observe, obtain engineering inspections, conduct environmental tests and inspections and any other
reasonable activity to determine the physical and financial condition of the Property. Old Operator
shall not unreasonably withhold or delay consent to any such Access. New Operator shall use
commercially reasonable efforts in connection with all such inspections pursuant to the Access to
avoid attention of staff of the Facilities and to avoid interference with services rendered to residents
of the Facilities. New Operator agrees to promptly repair any damage to the Property caused by
New Operator’s entry upon the Property. New Operator shall not make any invasive testing
without the written consent of the Old Operator, which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld.
Old Operator may require that a representative of Old Operator be present at the time of any
invasive testing conducted during the Access.

                b.      In conducting any inspections, investigations or tests of the Property, New
Operator shall (i) not unreasonably disturb the Facility or interfere with the use of the Property;
(ii) not materially or unreasonably interfere with the operation and maintenance of the Property;

           52

(iii) not materially damage any part of the Property or any personal property owned or held by any
tenant or any third party; (iv) not injure or otherwise cause bodily harm to Old Operator or its

comply in all material respects with all applicable laws; (vi) not permit any liens to attach to the
Property by reason of the exercise of its rights hereunder; and (vii) pay all costs and expenses
necessary to so that the Property can be used as it was used prior to such inspection, investigation,
or tests.

substantially all of its assets, (iv) suffered the attachment or judicial seizure of all, or substantially
all, of its assets, (v) admitted in writing its inability to pay its debts as they come due, or (vi) made
an offer of settlement, extension or composition of its creditors generally.

                c.       To New Operator’s knowledge, there is no condition, past or current
conduct, actions or omissions, or assertions or investigations relating thereto, of or related to the
New Operator or any of its affiliates (as such term is broadly defined under the Federal Securities
Laws (“Affiliate”)) why the Closing Approvals will not be granted.
                d.      New Operator has conducted its own independent investigation, review and
analysis of the Assets, and acknowledges that it has been provided adequate access to the
personnel, properties, assets, premises, books and records, and other documents and data of Old
Operator for such purpose. New Operator acknowledges and agrees that: (a) in making its decision
to enter into this Agreement and to consummate the transactions contemplated hereby, New
Operator has relied solely upon its own investigation and the express representations, warranties,
covenants and obligations of Old Operator set forth in this Agreement, and (b) neither Old Operator
nor any other Person has made any representation or warranty as to Old Operator or the Assets
except as set forth in this Agreement and the Purchase Agreement.

        10.    Old Operator’s Obligation Pending Closing. From the Effective Date through
the Closing Date, unless this Agreement is earlier terminated, Old Operator covenants to perform
the following obligations:

                a.      Old Operator shall continue to operate and maintain the Property and the
Facility as a skilled nursing facility, in accordance with its respective licenses and Permits and
consistent with its operation and maintenance at the time of execution of this Agreement. In this
respect, Old Operator shall (1) comply with all material regulations, lawful orders and directives
of all Governmental Authorities with respect to the Facility and the Property; (2) continue to
comply and perform in accordance with the material terms of all of the agreements which relate to
the Property and the Facility, the Service Contracts, any provider agreements and the Admission
Agreements; (3) continue to advertise and offer the Facility’s space for residency in a manner
consistent with past practices; (4) continue to manage the Facility by billing for and collecting
charges and otherwise enforcing the Admission Agreements in a manner consistent with past
practices; (5) take all commercially reasonable actions consistent with past practices intended to
preserve the present residency occupancy levels and payor mix at the Facility; (6) retain the
goodwill with all of the suppliers, clientele, residents and others having business relations with
Old Operator and the Facility in a manner consistent with past practices; (7) continue to hire
employees to the extent necessary to operate the Facility in accordance with past practices,
provided that Old Operator shall not hire any new administrator, director of nursing, director of
sales or the director of marketing without the approval of the New Operator, which consent may
not be withheld conditioned or delayed; (8) not decrease any rates of private pay residents of the
Facility without the prior written consent of New Operator; (9) maintain, repair and replace the
Property in a manner consistent with past practices so that the Property is in working order and in
substantially the same condition as at the Effective Date, subject to ordinary wear and tear; (10)
maintain existing insurance coverage until Closing, and advise New Operator of any material
change in coverage; (11) not remove any of the Assets from the Facility unless the same is replaced
by Assets of a substantially like kind and of equal or higher value; (12) not admit residents who
have no confirmed payor source and shall not implement its charity care program; and (13) not
take any action which would materially reduce the New Operator’s reimbursement rate after
Closing.

               b.       Old Operator will not, without New Operator’s prior written consent,
modify or amend any of the agreements which relate to the Property or the Facility or enter into
or renew any new Service Contracts or other agreements that are not cancelable at Closing. Old
Operator shall not create or allow to be created any liens, encumbrances, covenants, restrictions or
easements affecting or encumbering the Property which are not disclosed in this Agreement. Old
Operator will not sell, assign or convey any right, title, or interest whatever in the Property to any
third party other than Admission Agreements in the form previously submitted to the New
Operator which are entered into in the normal course of business.

              c.      Old Operator shall cure all violations and other deficiencies identified by
any Governmental Authority (all such matters collectively the “Violations”) relating to the Facility
and the Property, pay all penalties associated therewith and prepare and implement any Plan of

           52

Correction required by any Governmental Authority. In the event at Closing there are uncorrected
Violations relating to the Facility for which Old Operator has had insufficient time to cure, or any
Governmental Authority survey or inspection conducted prior to the Closing Date identifies any
Violations at the Facility which Old Operator has had insufficient time to cure, New Operator shall
proceed to Closing so long as all Violations are from Rhode Island Department of Health
(“RIDOH”) and relate to the provision of care to the residents at the Facility and none of the
Violations are of a material nature. Violations of a material nature includes (1) any Violation
which is a level G or higher, (2) any notice of intent to revoke or limit any Facility license or
Medicare/Medicaid certification, or (3) any restriction on reimbursements or new admission (each
of the above a “High Level Deficiency”). New Operator shall not be required to proceed to
Closing in the event that at any time prior to Closing there are one or more High Level Deficiencies
outstanding at the Facility or if there are Violations affecting the Property or the Facility that are
not from the RIDOH and in such event, New Operator shall have the option to either (i) adjourn
the Closing Date for a specified time to allow the Old Operator time to correct such Violations,
provided, that if the Violations are not fully cured and cleared to the satisfaction of the relevant
Government Authority within such specified time, New Operator shall have the right to terminate
this Agreement or can further postpone the Closing for another specified time in accordance with
the previous clause, and provided further, that no such postponement shall occur if it shall cause
DOH not to issue a new license to New Operator effective as of the Closing Date; or (ii) terminate
this Agreement.

           17.        Closing Prorations

                (a)     At Closing, Old Operator shall transfer or credit to New Operator, all of Old
Operator’s right, title and interest in and to all deposits required to be held under the Admission
Agreements, resident trust accounts, patient trust funds security and other deposits and any private
pay payments for services to be rendered subsequent to the Closing Date, if any (plus interest
thereon to the extent earned or required to be paid, if any) and no additional payment or adjustment
shall be made therefor.

                (b)     At the Closing, New Operator and Old Operator shall adjust for rents and
other charges collected, utilities, assessments, water and sewer service as of the Closing Date,
Service Contracts to the extent assumed by the New Operator and other customary prorations. The
parties shall prorate any signing or other bonus received by the Old Operator for any of the Service
Contracts or other agreements being assumed by the New Operator. Old Operator shall obtain
final meter readings immediately prior to Closing. In the event final meter readings are not
available, readings shall be estimated based on the most recent reading and the parties shall agree
to reconcile any discrepancies in the actual amount upon receipt of final readings, which obligation


           52
4841-2849-7870.5

===== PAGE 132 =====
shall survive closing. Old Operator shall provide the Title Company with any escrows required
for any lienable utilities.

                (c)    At New Operator’s election, at Closing Old Operator shall either (i) pay out
to all employees all accrued Employee Benefits for all Rehired Employees so that New Operator
shall have no obligations to any of the Rehired Employees for any Employee Benefits and in such
event, notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, New Operator shall have no liability
to any Rehired Employees for any matters related to prior to Closing, or (ii) provide a credit to
New Operator in the amount of all accrued Employee Benefits for all Rehired Employees and such
credit shall also include all associated taxes and other amounts which New Operator will be
required to pay in connection with the accrued Employee Benefits which include, but are not
limited to, FICA, federal unemployment taxes, social security, state payroll and other taxes related
to the foregoing (the “Payroll Taxes”). The parties agree to calculate the amount of Payroll Taxes
based upon an estimate of 10% of the accrued Employee Benefits. Old Operator will pay out to
the Facility Employees all accrued paid time off which is required to be paid out in accordance
with applicable law.

                  (d)     The Closing shall become effective and New Operator shall take over the
operations of the Facility as of 12:01 AM on the day following the Closing Date (the “Effective
Time”) and, except as otherwise set forth in this Agreement, Old Operator shall be entitled to all
benefits and be responsible for all obligations of the Facility related to the period prior to the
Effective Time and the New Operator shall be entitled to the benefits and be responsible for the
liabilities related to the period from and after the Effective Time. All prorations shall be made as
of the Closing Date based upon the most current information then available. The parties agree that
except as provided for herein, to the extent final prorations for anything other than real estate taxes
cannot be determined because of lack of data, information or readings accurate as of the Closing
Date, the parties shall adjust any prorations made on the Closing Statement based on the best
information available and shall re-prorate such amounts when the information becomes available
but no later than sixty (60) days after the Closing. Both parties shall respond promptly to requests
for data, information or readings from the other and execute any additional addenda to the Closing
Statement as may be reasonably necessary to reflect any adjustments. This Paragraph shall survive
Closing.

           18.     Accounts Receivable

               a.       All payments related to services provided from and after the Effective Time
shall belong to the New Operator and all payments related to services provided prior to the
Effective Time shall belong to the Old Operator. To the extent that either party receives payments
which belong to the other party, it shall deliver said payment to the other party within five (5) days
of receipt and at least one time each month the parties shall deliver to each other a schedule of
amounts received together with the remittances for reconciliation.

               b.     Until the end of eighteen (18) months from the Closing Date (the
“Receivables Date”), all funds received which are not from Medicaid, Medicare or other third
party payor (the “Private Pay Receivables”) shall belong to the Old Operator or New Operator as
appropriate in each instance in which the payment is identifiable to services provided by Old
Operator or New Operator. Subsequent to Receivables Date, all Private Pay Receivables received

           52
4841-2849-7870.5

===== PAGE 133 =====
shall belong to the New Operator. Payments of Private Pay Receivables which are not identifiable
to a specific period which are received within sixty (60) days after the Closing Date shall belong
to the Old Operator and all such Private Pay Receivables received more than sixty (60) days after
the Closing Date shall belong to the New Operator until all Private Pay Receivables owed to New
Operator are current and then to Old Operator in the amount of any unpaid installments of Private
Pay Receivables owed to Old Operator as stated in the Schedule of Old Operator Accounts
Receivable delivered to New Operator at Closing. New Operator shall cooperate with Old
Operator in regards to pending approvals for reimbursement of services for residents at the Facility
as of the Closing Date.

                c.      In the event that Old Operator receives any stimulus funds from any Federal,
state or other government agency following the Closing Date, it shall deliver said funds to the New
Operator unless the agency providing such funds has issued guidance which specifically prohibits
the delivery of the stimulus funds to the New Operator.

                   d.    This Section 18 shall survive Closing.

           19.     Post-Closing Obligations.

                a.      Old Operator shall timely prepare and file or cause to be prepared and filed
all Medicare, Medicaid and third-party payor cost reports for periods prior to the Closing Date and
shall deliver copies of such reports to New Operator, not later than ten (10) days following their
timely filing with the appropriate governmental agency or payor.

               b.      For periods prior to the Closing, Old Operator agrees to fully cooperate with
New Operator in connection with any desk audit or full audit by any applicable governmental
authority or regulatory agency, including without limitation, providing New Operator with any
available necessary records, documentation, supporting schedules and personnel in order to
properly support the dollar figures, RUG rates and patient classifications/characterizations
contained in Old Operator’s cost reports so that New Operator’s Medicaid or Medicare
reimbursement can be maximized in accordance with applicable law.

                  c.      Old Operator agrees that for a period of one (1) year after the Closing Date,
Old Operator, or any of its related or affiliated entities shall not (i) solicit or hire any of the
employees who are employed at any time after the date which is thirty (30) days before the
Effective Date until six (6) months after such employee is no longer working for New Operator,
or (ii) solicit or accept any residents of the Facility who are residents as of the Closing Date in any
other facility owned or operated by the Old Operator or any affiliate. Old Operator acknowledges
that New Operator’s damages for Old Operator’s breach of this provision are difficult to ascertain
and in the event Old Operator hires any such employee or admits any resident without New
Operator’s written consent, New Operator shall be entitled to liquidated damages in the amount of
Twenty Five Thousand Dollars ($25,000) for each employee so hired or resident so admitted. In
addition, Old Operator acknowledges that damages may not be sufficient to reimburse New

warranties of New Operator herein; (ii) any default by New Operator in the performance of any of
its commitments, covenants or obligations under this Agreement, (iii) any Assumed Liabilities.

                d.      If any party (the “Indemnified Party”) receives notice of the assertion or
commencement of any action, suit, claim or other legal proceeding made or brought by any person
who is not a party to this Agreement or an affiliate of a party to this Agreement or a representative
of the foregoing (a "Third-Party Claim") against such Indemnified Party with respect to which
the Indemnifying Party is obligated to provide indemnification under this Agreement, the
Indemnified Party shall give the Indemnifying Party prompt written notice thereof. The failure to
give such prompt written notice shall not, however, relieve the Indemnifying Party of its
indemnification obligations, except and only to the extent that the Indemnifying Party forfeits
rights or defenses by reason of such failure. Such notice by the Indemnified Party shall describe
the Third-Party Claim in reasonable detail, shall include copies of all material written evidence
thereof and shall indicate the estimated amount, if reasonably practicable, of the Loss that has been
or may be sustained by the Indemnified Party. The Indemnifying Party shall have the right to
participate in, or by giving written notice to the Indemnified Party, to assume the defense of any
Third-Party Claim at the Indemnifying Party's expense and by the Indemnifying Party's own
counsel, and the Indemnified Party shall cooperate in good faith in such defense. In the event that
the Indemnifying Party assumes the defense of any Third-Party Claim, subject to Section 20d, it
shall have the right to take such action as it deems necessary to avoid, dispute, defend, appeal or
make counterclaims pertaining to any such Third-Party Claim in the name and on behalf of the
Indemnified Party. The Indemnified Party shall have the right, at its own cost and expense, to
participate in the defense of any Third-Party Claim with counsel selected by it subject to the
Indemnifying Party's right to control the defense thereof. If the Indemnifying Party elects not to

           52

compromise or defend such Third-Party Claim or fails to promptly notify the Indemnified Party in
writing of its election to defend as provided in this Agreement, the Indemnified Party may, subject

and all Losses based upon, arising from or relating to such Third-Party Claim. Seller and Purchaser
shall cooperate with each other in all reasonable respects in connection with the defense of any
Third-Party Claim, including making available records relating to such Third-Party Claim and
furnishing, without expense (other than reimbursement of actual out-of-pocket expenses) to the
defending party, management employees of the non-defending party as may be reasonably
necessary for the preparation of the defense of such Third-Party Claim.

                 e.      Notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, the Indemnifying
Party shall not enter into settlement of any Third-Party Claim without the prior written consent of
the Indemnified Party (which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed), except as
provided in this Section 20(e). If a firm offer is made to settle a Third-Party Claim without leading
to liability or the creation of a financial or other obligation on the part of the Indemnified Party
and provides, in customary form, for the unconditional release of each Indemnified Party from all
liabilities and obligations in connection with such Third-Party Claim and the Indemnifying Party
desires to accept and agree to such offer, the Indemnifying Party shall give written notice to that
effect to the Indemnified Party. If the Indemnified Party fails to consent to such firm offer within
ten days after its receipt of such notice, the Indemnified Party may continue to contest or defend
such Third-Party Claim and in such event, the maximum liability of the Indemnifying Party as to
such Third-Party Claim shall not exceed the amount of such settlement offer. If the Indemnified
Party fails to consent to such firm offer and also fails to assume defense of such Third-Party Claim,
the Indemnifying Party may settle the Third-Party Claim upon the terms set forth in such firm offer
to settle such Third-Party Claim. If the Indemnified Party has assumed the defense pursuant to
Section 20c, it shall not agree to any settlement without the written consent of the Indemnifying
Party (which consent shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed).

                 f.     Any claim by an Indemnified Party on account of a Loss which does not
result from a Third-Party Claim (a "Direct Claim") shall be asserted by the Indemnified Party
giving the Indemnifying Party prompt written notice thereof. The failure to give such prompt
written notice shall not, however, relieve the Indemnifying Party of its indemnification obligations,
except and only to the extent that the Indemnifying Party forfeits rights or defenses by reason of
such failure. Such notice by the Indemnified Party shall describe the Direct Claim in reasonable
detail, shall include copies of all material written evidence thereof and shall indicate the estimated
amount, if reasonably practicable, of the Loss that has been or may be sustained by the Indemnified
Party. The Indemnifying Party shall have thirty (30) days after its receipt of such notice to respond
in writing to such Direct Claim. During such thirty (30) day period, the Indemnified Party shall
allow the Indemnifying Party and its professional advisors to investigate the matter or
circumstance alleged to give rise to the Direct Claim, and whether and to what extent any amount
is payable in respect of the Direct Claim and the Indemnified Party shall assist the Indemnifying
Party's investigation by giving such information and assistance (including access to the
Indemnified Party's premises and personnel and the right to examine and copy any accounts,
documents or records) as the Indemnifying Party or any of its professional advisors may
reasonably request. If the Indemnifying Party does not so respond within such thirty (30) day
period, the Indemnifying Party shall be deemed to have rejected such claim, in which case the

               b.      If Old Operator defaults under this Agreement which shall include a

with any of its obligations set forth in this Agreement, the New Operator may terminate this
Agreement after the provision of notice by New Operator to Old Operator and ten (10) days
opportunity to cure said default by Old Operator, the New Operator may terminate this Agreement
and pursue all rights and remedies in law or equity. In addition, Old Operator agrees that
irreparable damage may occur in the event that any of the provisions of this Agreement were not
performed in accordance with their specific terms or were otherwise breached by Old Operator. It
is accordingly agreed that New Operator shall be entitled to an injunction or injunctions to prevent
breaches of this Agreement by Old Operator and to enforce specifically the representations,

epidemic, computer virus effecting any governmental authority which is required for the Closing,
natural disaster, power or other mechanical failure, any material disruption in the financial,
banking or securities markets, or any condition which causes lenders to pull lending commitments.

               b. In the event of any such excused delay, the time for performance of such
obligations shall be extended for a period equal to the time lost by reason of the delay plus fifteen
(15) days, provided, however, that Old Operator shall not have the right to adjourn the Closing
Date for more than thirty (30) days. In the event that the delay, frustration, hindrance, or delay
caused by the Force Majeure set forth herein lasts for a period of more than sixty (60) days or is
reasonably likely to last for more than 60 days, Purchaser shall have the right to terminate this
Agreement.
