                                           OPERATING AGREEMENT
                                                    OF
                                         TRYKO NEXGEN HOLDINGS LLC

       THIS OPERATING AGREEMENT (the “Agreement”) is made and entered into as of January 25, 2021 by YR
NEXGEN TRUST, SK NEXGEN TRUST and YK NEXGEN TRUST (individually “Member” and collectively
“Members”).
         In consideration of the mutual covenants herein contained, the Members hereby enter into this Operating
Agreement to control the operations of Tryko Nexgen Holdings LLC, a limited liability company formed under and
pursuant to the laws of the State of New Jersey (hereinafter sometimes referred to as the “Company”) for the purposes
and upon the terms, provisions and conditions hereinafter set forth.
         Unless the context shall clearly indicate otherwise, all words and phrases used in this Operating Agreement
shall have the meaning set forth in the New Jersey Limited Liability Company Act, (the “Act”).
                                                      ARTICLE 1
                                                     FORMATION
         1.   Name. The name of the Company shall be Tryko Nexgen Holdings LLC.
        2. Place of Business. The principal place of business of the Company shall be 1608 Route 88, Suite 200,
Brick, New Jersey or such other place or places as the Manager may from time to time designate.
          3. Formation; Ratification. The Company was formed by the filing of the Articles of Organization on
January 25, 2021. The Members do hereby ratify and authorize any and all actions taken in the name of the Company
since the date of formation and acknowledge that this Agreement shall control the operations of the Company from the
date of its formation.
          4. Member’s Authority. Except as otherwise expressly and specifically provided for in this Agreement or in
the Certificate of Formation, no Member shall have any authority to act for, or assume any obligations or responsibility
on behalf of, the Company or any other Member of the Company.
        5. Member’s Prior Obligations. No Member shall be responsible or liable for any obligation or debt of any
other Member incurred before the formation of the Company except as may be set forth herein, and each party
indemnifies and agrees to hold all others harmless from such obligations and indebtedness.
        6. Other Business. Nothing herein shall restrict any Member from conducting any other business or activity
whatsoever (including any operation or business or activity which competes with the business of the Company).
         7. Registered Agent. The registered agent for the Company shall be Business Services Plus LLC with an
address of 1608 Route 88, Suite 200, Brick, NJ 08724.
         8. Term. The Company shall continue until the earliest to occur of the sale or other disposition of all or
substantially all of the property of the Company, the incompetence or permanent disability of the sole remaining
Member or upon the approval of the Manager and three-quarters (3/4) of all of the Membership Interests, at least
coextensive with the remaining term of the Mortgage.
          9. Purposes of the Company. The purpose of the Company shall be to engage in any business in which a
limited liability company may lawfully engage in the State of New Jersey.
          10. Powers of the Company. The Company shall at any time and from time to time have the power and
authority to do or cause to be done all things necessary or desirable to carry out the purposes of the Company described
in Section 1(9) hereof, including, but not limited to acquiring or disposing of real or personal (or mixed) property or any
interest therein and manage property for others, obtaining financing for the Company, including refinancing and further
including interim and permanent financing (and refinancing) for the acquisition and development of real property and
financing the operations of the Company, sell or transfer or mortgage or place (or suffer to be placed) an encumbrance
on any property of the Company, enter into leases or other similar arrangements, construct, reconstruct, alter, maintain,
repair and demolish improvements on or to any real property of the Company, enter into contracts with others to perform
or carry out any or all of the foregoing and to do all other things necessary or desirable to conduct the business of the
Company and to carry out the terms and conditions of this Agreement.

                                                     ARTICLE 2

        1. Certain Definitions. When used in this Agreement, the following terms shall have the meanings set forth

         as the same may be amended from time to time.
                (b) “Capital Account” as of any given date shall mean the Capital Contributions to the Company by a

                  (c) “Capital Contribution” shall mean any contribution to the capital of the Company in cash or

         purchase or other acquisition by such person of interest, that person shall have all the rights of a Member with
         respect to the purchased or otherwise acquired Membership Interest.
                  (g) “Membership Interest” shall mean each Member’s percentage interest in the Company.

beyond the Member’s respective Capital Contributions and any obligation of the Member to make Capital Contributions,

                                                  ARTICLE 4
                                            CAPITAL CONTRIBUTIONS
          1. Capital Contributions. All Capital Contributions of the Company shall be contributed in accordance
with the Membership Interests of the Members. The Manager does not, in any way, guarantee the return of the
Members’ Capital Contributions or a profit for the Members from the operations of the Company. All Capital
Contributions of the Manager shall be entitled to a preferred return of ten (10%) percent. In addition to the Capital
Contributions of the Members, upon acquisition of the Company’s property, the Company may borrow additional funds
from all of the Members or an affiliate of the Company which loan shall be evidenced by a note and shall be repaid as set
forth therein.

          2. Estimated Expenses. The Manager shall, from time to time, but not more often than quarterly, estimate
the cash requirements of the Company for an ensuing period of time and the Members shall each be responsible to pay
their pro rata share of such estimated cash requirements in accordance with the Members’ Membership Interests.
          3. Additional Capital Contributions. Upon demand by the Manager, each Member agrees to pay, within
five (5) days after receiving a demand therefore, its pro rata share (based upon such Member’s interest in the Company
pursuant to Section 3(1) and irrespective of the percentage of the Initial Capital Contributions of each Member) of all
amounts demanded by the Manager for whatever purpose. The provisions of this paragraph shall be binding upon the
Members and the Company, but such provisions shall not inure to the benefit of any other person including any creditors
of the Company. Any distribution due to a Member who has defaulted in payment of any required Capital Contribution
shall be first applied to any reimbursement owed by that defaulting Member to the Company plus interest at the rate of
twenty four (24%) percent or the highest amount allowed by law whichever is less, which shall compound monthly,
pursuant to the heter iska in Section 9(12) below (the “Heter Iska”), or to another Member who has made the Capital
Contributions on the part of the defaulting Member plus interest as provided for in Section 5 of this Article, before
distribution of the remainder, if any, to the defaulting Member and such distribution to the Member who has made the
Capital Contribution shall be made from all cash flows regardless of whether or not from the project in which the
Member failed to make a capital contribution.
          4. Option to Assist Defaulting Member. If any Member shall default in the payment of any sums required
to be paid by it to the Company hereunder (the “Defaulting Member”), the Manager shall notify the other Members
regarding such default and the other Members may (the “Assisting Member”), without the Company waiving any
claims for breach, cure such default for the account of the Defaulting Member (the “Default Loan”). The Defaulting
Member shall be responsible to repay (solely out of any distribution to which the defaulting Member may be entitled) the
Default Loan with interest at the lesser of twenty four (24%) percent or the highest amount allowed by law which shall
compound monthly and shall be subject to the Heter Iska. Until the Default Loan is repaid in full, with interest as herein
provided, all distributions which would otherwise have been made to the Defaulting Member shall be paid to the
Assisting Member, to be applied first to accrued interest and then to principal and the Company shall repay the Default
Loan with interest, and notwithstanding the provisions of Section 6 of Article 6, such repayment shall be made from all
cash flow regardless of whether or not such funds are from the project in which the Defaulting Member failed to make a
contribution. Any distribution to the Assisting Member in connection with the Default Loan pursuant to this Paragraph
(or to the Company pursuant to Section 4 of this Article) shall be considered a distribution to the Defaulting Member. In
the event more than one Member wishes to make the Default Loan, such Members shall each make a loan to the
Defaulting Member pro rata based on the percentage interest each such Assisting Member has to the combined
Membership Interests of all Assisting Members. All amounts contributed by the Assisting Members pursuant to this
Section shall be paid within ten (10) days of the delivery by the Manager of the notice of default. In the event that the
Default Loan is not paid within ninety (90) days, the Assisting Member shall have the right to purchase the Membership
Interests of the Defaulting Member in an amount equal to three times the value represented by the Default Loan as it
relates to the total amount of capital contributions to the Company.
         5. Priority of Capital Contributions. Except as may be expressly provided in this Agreement, no Member
shall have priority over any other Member, either for the return of Capital Contributions or for net profits, net losses, or



                                                             3

===== PAGE 253 =====
distributions; provided that this Section shall not apply to loans (as distinguished from Capital Contributions) which a
Member has made to the Company.
         6. Loans. The Manager shall have the right to borrow funds on behalf of the Company at the best available
terms, including from any Member, at any time that the Company needs funds for the continued operations. Any loan
from a Member shall accrue interest from the Company at the then Applicable Federal Rate announced by the Internal
Revenue Service plus eighteen (18%) percent per annum (but in no event more than 24% or the highest amount allowed
by law) pursuant to the Heter Iska and any payments on such loan shall be first used to pay interest and then to principal.


                                                    ARTICLE 5
                                                   MANAGEMENT
         1. Management. The management of the Company shall be by a Manager, who shall be the only Member
empowered to bind the Company. Except as specifically set forth to the contrary herein, the Manager shall have the
authority to make all decisions for the Company and all decisions of the Manager shall be binding upon the Company,
except as set forth in subsection 3.
         2. Certain Powers of Manager. Subject to the provisions of subsection 3, the Manager shall have the
exclusive power and authority, on behalf of the Company, in accordance with Section 1 above:
                 (a) To approve the sale, exchange, mortgage or other disposition of all, or substantially all, of the
         Company’s assets which is to occur as part of a single transaction or plan and to sell and acquire property to and
         from any person or entity (“Person”) as part of an arm’s length transaction as such Manager may determine and
         upon commercially reasonable terms.
                    (b) To borrow for the Company from banks, other lending institutions, Persons, Members, or
         affiliates of the Manager or Members on such terms as the Manager deems appropriate so long as such terms
         are commercially reasonable, and in connection therewith, to hypothecate, encumber, and grant security
         interests in the assets of the Company to secure repayment of the borrowed sums. No debt shall be contracted
         or liability incurred by or on behalf of the Company except by the Manager or, to the extent permitted under the
         Act, by agents or employees of the Company expressly authorized to contract such debt or to incur such
         liability by the Manager;
                  (c) To purchase liability and other insurance to protect the Company’s property and business;
                  (d) To hold and own any Company real and/or personal properties in the name of the Company;
                  (e) To invest in and/or acquire and operate any business;
                  (f) To time open bank accounts in the name of the Company, and the Manager shall be the sole
         signatory thereon, unless the Manager determines otherwise.
                   (g) To invest any Company funds temporarily (by way of example but not limitation) in time
         deposits, short-term governmental obligations, commercial paper, or other investments;
                    (h) To execute on behalf of the Company all instruments and documents, including, without
         limitation: checks; drafts; notes and other negotiable instruments; mortgages, or deeds of trust; security
         agreements; financing statements; documents providing for the acquisition, mortgage or disposition of the
         Company’s property; assignments; bills of sale; leases; Company agreements; operating agreements of other
         entities; and any other instruments or documents necessary, in the opinion of the Manager, to the business of
         the Company;
                   (i) To employ accountants, legal counsel, managing agents, brokers, or other experts to perform
         services for the Company and to compensate them from Company funds. The fact that a Manager or a Member
         is directly or indirectly affiliated or connected with any such employed party shall not prohibit the Manager
         from employing that party so long as the terms of such employment are commercially reasonable;
                  (j) To enter into any and all other agreements on behalf of the Company, with any person for any
         purpose, in such forms as the Manager may approve;
                   (k) To act as an attorney in fact under any power of attorney given to the Company by any person or
         entity; and


                                                            4

                 (l) To perform any capital improvements, rehabilitation or other work at the Property as determined
         by the Manager and to execute any contracts in connection with same.
                  (m) To make capital calls and to maintain reserves for the Company.
                (n) To do and perform all other acts as may be necessary or appropriate to the conduct of the
         Company’s business.
                  (o) YR Nexgen Trust is the Manager ("Manager") of this Company which is empowered by and
         under this Agreement to acquire from and sell property to any person or entity upon commercially
         reasonable terms as such Manager may determine without authorization from the other Members of this
         Company. Yitzchok Rokowsky is hereby appointed as the managing director of the Company and it is
         acknowledged by all Members, that Mr. Rokowsky is empowered by and under this Agreement with all of
         the powers of the Manager and that Mr. Rokowsky can make all decisions for the Company set forth herein
         for the Manager and shall execute on behalf of this Company all instruments necessary to effectuate the
         acquisition or sale of property from or to any person or entity, whether such property is owned by the
         Company or through a power of attorney, without the authorization of the other Members of this Company
         and to do all acts necessary for the operation of the Company. The managing director may appoint others
         to execute any specific document by a written resolution of the Company containing such authorization.
         3. Restrictions on Manager. Notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, from the Effective
Date and until the date on which Yitzchok Rokowsky ceases to serve as the Managing Director, the Managing Director
shall not have the right to determine the amount and frequency of any distributions from the Company and such
determinations shall be made by the “Distribution Manager.” Tzvi Levovitz is hereby designated as the Distribution
Manager, but if Tzvi Levovitz is unable or unwilling to serve as the Distribution Manager during such period, then any
other Person appointed by a majority approval of the Members shall serve as the Distribution Manager.

         4. Reserved Powers. Unless authorized to do so by this Agreement or by the Manager of the Company,
no attorney-in-fact, employee, or other agent of the Company shall have any power or authority to bind the
Company in any way, to pledge its credit or to render it liable peculiarly for any purpose. No Member shall have
any power or authority to bind the Company unless the Member is the Manager or has been authorized by the
Manager to act as an agent of the Company in accordance herewith.
          5. Liability for Certain Acts. The Manager shall perform its duties in good faith, in a manner it reasonably
believes to be in the best interest of the Company, and with such care as an ordinarily prudent person in a like position
would use under similar circumstances. The Manager who so performs the duties of Manager shall not have any liability
by reason of being or having been a Manager of the Company. The Manager does not, in any way, guarantee the return
of the Members’ Capital Contributions or a profit for the member from the operations of the Company. The Manager
shall not be liable to the Company or to any Member for any loss or damage sustained by the Company for any reason.




                                                           5

===== PAGE 255 =====
           6. Indemnity. To the fullest extent permitted by the Act, the Company shall indemnify, defend and hold
harmless each person and entity (an "Indemnified Person") who was or is made a party or is threatened to be made a
party to or is involved in any threatened, pending or completed action, suit or proceeding, whether civil, criminal,
administrative, arbitrative or investigative (including, without limitation, an action by or in the right of the Company), or
any appeal in such a proceeding or any inquiry or investigation that could lead to such a proceeding, by reason of the fact
that he, or a person or entity of whom he is the personal representative, is or was an officer, Manager, Member,
employee or agent of the Company, or is or was serving as a director, member, manager, officer, partner, venturer,
proprietor, trustee, employee or agent of another limited liability company, corporation, partnership, joint venture, trust,
sole proprietorship, employee benefit plan or other enterprise, that is or was a Manager or Member or which has any
direct or indirect interest in the Company or any manager and all affiliates of such Indemnified Person and any entity,
whether or not affiliated with such Indemnified Person, that a judgment against such entity will have an adverse effect on
any Indemnified Person, from and against any liabilities, expenses (including, without limitation, attorneys' fees and
expenses and any other costs and expenses incurred in connection with defending such action, suit or proceeding),
judgments, penalties, fines and amounts paid in settlement actually and reasonably incurred by such Indemnified Person
in connection with such proceeding except that the provisions of this Section shall not be applicable to any liability
which is the result of a Bad Act. The Company shall indemnify its Manager and its agents from any claims alleging a
breach of duty to the Company or its Members unless it is determined that an act or omission was intentionally (a) in
breach of such Person’s duty of loyalty to the Company or its Members (b) not in good faith or involving a knowing
violation of law or (c) resulted in the receipt of an improper benefit by such Person. Company shall name the foregoing
entities and individuals for whom it is required to indemnify as additional insureds on its general and professional
liability insurance.

           7. Manager Has No Exclusive Duty to Company. The Manager shall not be required to manage the
Company as its sole and exclusive function and it may have other business interests and may engage in other activities in
addition to those relating to the Company including any activities which directly compete with the Company. Neither
the Company nor any Member shall have any right, by virtue of this Agreement, to share or participate in such other
investments or activities of the Manager or to the income or proceeds derived therefrom. The Manager shall incur no
liability to the Company or to any of the Members as a result of engaging in any other business or venture. The Manager
shall have the right, on commercially reasonable terms, to hire an affiliate to perform all capital repairs, maintenance,
construction, rehabilitation and any other work at the Property or to perform such repairs, maintenance, construction,
rehabilitation or other work on its own, but shall have no obligation to do so.

       8. Additional Members. At any time, the Manager shall have the right to admit additional members to the
Company upon any terms determined by the Manager in its sole discretion.
         9. Reimbursements. The Company will reimburse the Manager or its affiliate for all actual and reasonable
costs and expenses incurred in the course of its service to the Company hereunder including any salary and other
compensation paid to its employees who work at the property and who perform legal, financial analysis and bookkeeping
work. The Manager shall be entitled to a management fee of one million dollars per year.
         10. Resignation. The Manager may resign at any time by giving written notice to the Members of the
Company. The resignation of the Manager shall take effect upon receipt of the notice or at such later time as shall be
specified in the notice; and, unless otherwise specified in the notice, the acceptance of the resignation shall not be
necessary to make it effective. The resignation of a Manager who is also a Member shall not affect the Manager’s rights
as a Member and shall not constitute a withdrawal of a Member.
          11. Removal and Replacement. The Manager may be removed upon an affirmative vote of Members
owning more than a majority of the Membership Interests of the Company. Any vacancy occurring for any reason in the
office of Manager for any reason shall be filled by the affirmative vote of a majority of the Members. A Manager
elected to fill a vacancy shall be elected for the unexpired term of the Manager’s predecessor in office and shall hold
office until a successor shall be elected and shall qualify, or until the Manager’s earlier death, resignation, or removal.
The managing director or other officer may be removed by the Manager or by a majority of the members at any time and
in such event the Manager or majority of the members shall appoint a new managing director or other officer.
                                               ARTICLE 6
                                      ALLOCATIONS AND DISTRIBUTIONS




                                                             6

===== PAGE 256 =====
         1. Allocation of Profits. Allocations of profit shall be made as follows: (a) items of net income and gain
shall be allocated among the Members in the manner necessary to increase each Member’s Capital Account to an
amount equal to the amount of cash such Member would be entitled to receive pursuant to subsections (6) of this
Article if an amount of cash equal to the net positive Capital Account balances (after such allocation and after
hypothetically increasing such Capital Account balances by amounts of any Company Minimum Gain and Member
Minimum Gain that would be allocated to the Members if all Company assets were sold for an amount equal to the
Company indebtedness encumbering such assets) were distributed to the Members in the order and priority specified
in subsections (6) of this Article,
         2. Allocation of Losses. Items of net loss and deduction shall be allocated among the Members in the
manner necessary to reduce each Member’s Capital Account to an amount equal to the amount of cash such Member
would be entitled to receive pursuant to subsections (6) of this Article if an amount of cash equal to the net positive
Capital Account balances after such allocation and after hypothetically increasing such Capital Account balances by
amounts of any Company Minimum Gain and Member Minimum Gain that would be allocated to the Members if all
Company assets were sold for an amount equal to the Company indebtedness encumbering such assets) were distributed
to the Members in the order and priority specified in subsections (6) of this Article.
          3. Other Allocations. It is the intention of the Members that allocations of profits and losses made to the
Members have economic effect equivalence in accordance with the regulations under Internal Revenue Code Section
704(b). The provisions of Section 6(1) and Section 6(2) shall be interpreted consistent with, and subject to, the terms
set forth on Exhibit A attached hereto, which terms shall be incorporated herein as if restated in full in this Article 6,
as applicable to the operations of the Company.
         4.   Capital Accounts. Each Member shall have a Capital Account which shall consist of its
                  (a) Capital Contributions to the capital of the Company;
                  (b) Plus, the amount of profits allocated to it pursuant to Section 6(1);
                  (c) Minus, the amount of losses associated to it pursuant to this Article 6(2); and
                  (d) Minus, all amounts distributed to or on behalf of such Member pursuant hereto;
         5. Interest on Capital Accounts. No interest shall be paid or accrued on any Member’s Capital Account
except as herein provided.
         6. Distributions. All distributions of the Company shall be distributed to the Members based on each
investment made by the Company in the order of priority set forth below so that the distributions for each investment
shall be made based on the following waterfall (after the repayment of any Default Loans) regardless of whether or not
all cumulative capital contributions have been returned to the Members:
                  a)   to the Members in accordance with and in proportion to each Member’s accrued but undistributed
                       Preferred Distribution, if any;
                  b) to the Members in accordance with and in proportion to each Member’s capital contribution in
                     the Company until each Member receives the total amount of its capital contributions;
                  c)   to the Manager in the amount of twenty five percent and seventy five percent of all distributions
                       shall be distributed to the Members in accordance with the Members’ Membership Interests in the
                       Company as provided in Section 3(1).
Prior to any distribution of cash flow arising from the sale or disposition of all or substantially all of the Company’s
property, the Company shall first apply such cash flow to the payment of any unpaid expenses and outstanding loans of
the Company prior to the distributions set forth in this subsection.
                                                  ARTICLE 7
                                             TRANSFER OF INTEREST




                                                            7

===== PAGE 257 =====
          1. Prohibition Against Transfer. Except as hereinafter set forth, no Member may sell, assign, transfer,
encumber or otherwise dispose of all or any part of the property of the Company or its interest in the Company whether
direct or indirect through any number of tiers without the consent of the Manager which consent may be withheld in its
sole and absolute discretion. It is the intent of this section to maintain the control of the Company in the natural persons
who own the Company on the Effective Date, other than the Manager who may transfer its interests at any time. Any
transfer not in accordance with this Article 7 shall have no effect and will not be recognized by the Company. Upon the
death of any individual who has interest in the Company on the date that the Company initially acquires the property,
such interest shall devise to his heirs.

          2. Assignment by Members. Any Member shall have the right to transfer or assign all or part of its share to
a subsidiary or affiliated company or to an entity in which the Member is the Manager or controlling party with full
control of all aspects of the entity (hereinafter a “Permitted Assignee”). Any Member or any Permitted Assignee may
also transfer or assign part, but not all, of its or their share to individuals who control that Member or any Permitted
Assignee, to heir lineal descendants (hereinafter “Family Members”), or to any or all of the foregoing; provided,
however, transfers or assignments to Family Members shall not include any voting rights, which voting rights shall be
reserved by the Member or the Permitted Assignee which is making the transfer or assignment to the Family Member.
         3. Assumption by Assignee. Any assignee or transferee who acquires all or part of a share in the Company
pursuant to the terms of this Agreement or otherwise shall take such share subject to all of the terms and conditions of
this Agreement; and such person shall not be considered to have such share until he shall agree in writing to assume the
obligations of his assignor or transfer hereunder and if such person is not at the time of such transfer a party to this
Agreement, to otherwise be bound by the terms and conditions of this Agreement.
                                                      ARTICLE 8
                                                     DISSOLUTION
          1. Termination or Dissolution. Upon termination of the Company, the Company shall be dissolved and its
affairs and business shall be liquidated forthwith. All property of the Company shall be sold or otherwise disposed of
and the new proceeds therefor, if any, and the remaining assets of the Company shall be applied first to satisfy the costs,
expenses, charges and liabilities of the Company, either by payment or the establishment of an adequate reserve to cover
the same and the balance shall be distributed to the Members in accordance with the distribution provisions of this
Agreement.
         2. Tax Effect of Termination or Dissolution. It is the intention of the Members that allocations made to the
Members have economic effect equivalence in accordance with the regulations under Internal Revenue Code Section
704(b). The provisions of this Section 8(2) shall be interpreted consistent with, and subject to, the terms set forth on
Exhibit A attached hereto, which terms shall be incorporated herein as if restated in full in this Article 8, as
applicable to the termination and dissolution of the Company. If a Member has a deficit balance in its Capital
Account (after giving effect to all contributions, distributions, and allocations for all tax years, including the tax year
in which such liquidation occurs), such Member shall have no obligation to make any contribution to the capital of
the Company with respect to such deficit, and such deficit shall not be considered a debt owed to the Company or to
any other Person for any purpose whatsoever.
         3. Dissolution Procedure. Within thirty (30) days of the termination of the Company or an event of
Dissolution as set forth in this Agreement, the Manager shall retain the services of a qualified appraiser to value all of the
property of the Company. The assets of the Company shall be distributed to the Members if practical and if acceptable
to all Members, or sold at prices in accordance with said appraisal.
         4. Continuation. Nothing contained herein shall be construed as limiting the ability of the remaining
Members from execution of a new Agreement and continuing the business of the Company, all in accordance with the
Act and the applicable provision of the Code.
        5. Events of Dissolution. The Company shall dissolve and commence to wind up its affairs upon the
occurrence of any of the following, in addition to those set forth elsewhere herein (each a “Dissolution Event”):
                (a) The written consent of the manager and the members holding three quarters (3/4) of all of the
         Membership Interests to dissolve;
                   (b) The entry of a decree of judicial dissolution under Section 18-802 of the Act; or




                                                              8

===== PAGE 258 =====
                   (c) Ninety (90) days after the date on which the Company no longer has at least one Member, unless
         at least one new Member is admitted to the Company within such 90-day period by such Member’s legal
         representative.
         The Members hereby agree that, notwithstanding any provision of the Act, the Company shall not dissolve
         prior to the occurrence of a Dissolution Event. For avoidance of doubt, any event that terminates the continued
         membership of any Member shall not cause the Company to be dissolved or its affairs to be wound up, and
         upon the occurrence of any such event, the Company shall be continued without dissolution.

                                                     ARTICLE 9
                                                  MISCELLANEOUS
         1. Property Manager. Upon agreement of the Managers, a property management company shall be hired
for the management of any real property owned by the Company. Any property management agreement shall be in
writing, and shall include a provision that the agreement may be terminated upon one full calendar month’s written
notice. Any Member may have an ownership or controlling interest in the chosen property management company. The
Management Fee paid to the Manager shall be reduced by any amount paid to a third party management company.
           2. Members Consent. The Company is not required to hold any meetings. Whenever the consent or the
vote of the Members is required in this Agreement or by law, the Manager shall notify all of the Members of the need for
consent or a vote, which notice may be via email and a phone conversation to each Member. Such consent shall be
deemed granted and such vote shall be deemed approved upon the receipt by the Manager of the required amount of
affirmative email responses by Members representing the required amount of Membership Interests for such consent as
set forth in this Agreement. In the event that the percentage of the Company Interest required for a specific action is not
specified in this Agreement, such action shall be approved upon the consent of the majority of the Membership Interests.
         3. Notices. Except as may be otherwise specifically provided for in this Agreement, no notices, requests,
consent, approval, waiver or other communication under this Agreement shall be effective unless in writing and either
(a) personally served thereon or (b) sent by regular mail and by certified mail, return receipt requested, with postage
prepaid, or (c) commercial express delivery service providing delivery against receipt, addressed to the parties at the
addresses on file with the Manager, or (d) sent via email to the principal of the Member and such principal of the
Member receiving the notice sends a confirming email.. If personally served or sent via commercial delivery service,
any such matter shall be deemed given for all purposes hereunder at the time of such service or, if by mail as herein
provided, at the time of depositing same in a post office box regularly maintained by the United States Postal Service. If
such notice is sent via email then notice shall be deemed given upon the receipt of the confirming email.
         4. Indemnities. Each Member hereby indemnifies and holds harmless the Manager, the other Members, and
the Company from and against any losses, costs, damages or liabilities which said other Members or the Company, or
both, may suffer or incur as a result of the activities of the indemnifying Member, whether or not wrongful, outside the
scope of this Agreement.
          5. Headings. The headings used in this Agreement are used for administrative purposes only and do not
constitute substantive matter to be considered in construing the terms of this Agreement.
          6. Entire Agreement. This Agreement, together with the Certificate of Formation of the Company, contains
the entire understanding between the parties and supersedes any prior understandings or written or oral agreements
between them respecting the within subject matter.
         7. Parties Bound. This Agreement shall be binding upon and inure the benefit of the parties hereto and their
respective successors and assigns except as otherwise set forth herein.
         8. Gender. Wherever herein the singular number is used, the same shall include the plural, and the
masculine gender shall include the feminine and neuter genders, and vice versa, as the context shall require. This
Agreement may be executed in several counterparts, each of which shall be an original, but all of which shall constitute
one and the same instrument.
          9. New Jersey Law. The rights and remedies of the Members and Manager shall be construed in accordance
with the laws of the State of New Jersey.
         10. Construction. It is the expressed purpose of the parties hereto to have the Company, in all cases, for
federal and State income taxes, treated as a partnership. Therefore, in the event any Section of this Agreement shall be



                                                            9

===== PAGE 259 =====
subject to interpretation, in all events the interpretation placed upon this Agreement shall be the interpretation which
provided the most favorable tax treatment to the Company and the interpretation which will advance the purpose of
having the Company treated as a partnership.
          11. Ratification. The Members, by their execution hereof, hereby adopt and ratify all actions taken, liabilities
incurred, contracts executed, and agreements entered into prior to the date hereof by the Company via the actions of the
Manager and/or his designees.

           12. Heter Iska. The undersigned members of the Company hereby declare and admit that all advances made
between themselves with respect to capital contributions, loans to the company, capital calls, or any other mortgages,
guarantees, preferred investments, or any other advances, that would violate any of the laws of Ribbis shall be structured
as an Iska investment subject to the following terms. In exchange for the monies advanced, the recipient/beneficiary shall
transfer to the investor a share (of equal value) in his assets as an Iska. In the event that no such investment exists, or if
the recipient participates in a more lucrative investment opportunity in the future, the recipient shall assign a share of that
investment to the investor. This investment shall be owned by the investing partner, and all profits, capital gains, or
losses shall be his sole responsibility. The recipient shall have complete authority to manage this investment on behalf of
the investor, in the manner deemed most beneficial to the investor. Any investment owned by the recipient may be used
to create this Iska, provided that the venture is Halachacly permissible. This Iska may only be collected from the assets of
the Company and the investor shall have no claim against the personal assets of any of the recipients, unless otherwise
specified in an agreement between the parties. The recipient shall receive five percent of the profits as compensation for
his services managing this Iska, and shall be liable for the monies as a watchman. The recipient must verify any claim of
loss through the testimony of two Halachacly acceptable witnesses in an Orthodox Jewish court of law. Any claim
regarding the amount of profits generated by the joint venture must be verified with a solemn oath, administered by an
Orthodox Jewish court of law. In addition, the investor may demand full access to all legal records, documents, financial
statements or receipts that the investor deems necessary to verify the claims of the recipient. In the event of a claim of
loss, the investor may demand a full and immediate payment of the remaining balance of this Iska. It is agreed that if the
recipient pays the amount equal to the principal, interest, penalties, default rates, preferred returns, membership dilution,
buyout rights, or other benefits called for by this Agreement, as per the payment schedule of this Agreement, as payment
for the investor's share of the principal and profits of the Iska investment, the investor shall waive his right to demand
verification of the results of the investment. Any additional profits shall belong solely to the recipient. Such payment
shall reflect a final settlement, and the recipient shall have no further claim for compensation, management or services
fees, unless specified to the contrary in other agreements executed between the parties. Any payments made that are not
justified by this Iska agreement shall reduce the principal balance of this Iska. However, any future payments made to
avoid providing the abovementioned verification, shall be increased to compensate for any discrepancy. This section
shall override any other section, even those that do not reference this section. Any mention of loans or interest shall be
interpreted as an Iska and to payments mentioned above. This declaration is legally binding and may be enforced in any
Jewish or civil court, even in the event that one of the parties was unaware of its existence. This agreement has been
executed in an effective manner and with a proper Kinyan, if required.

         13. Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in counterparts which together shall constitute one fully
executed Agreement. This Agreement shall become effective upon the exchange of facsimile or emailed signatures. A
copy of any parties’ signature shall be the same as an original.

                                                 Exhibit A

                                              Tax Provisions

1.   Special Allocations. The following special allocations shall be made in the following order:

              (a)       Minimum Gain Chargeback. Except as otherwise provided in Regulations Section
     1.704-2(f), notwithstanding any other provision of this Exhibit, if there is a net decrease in Company
     Minimum Gain during any fiscal year, each Member shall be specially allocated items of Company income
     and gain for such fiscal year (and, if necessary, subsequent fiscal years) in an amount equal to such
     Member's share of the net decrease in Company Minimum Gain, determined in accordance with
     Regulations Section 1.704-2(g). Allocations pursuant to the previous sentence shall be made in proportion
     to the respective amounts required to be allocated to each Member pursuant thereto. The items to be so
     allocated shall be determined in accordance with Regulations Sections 1.704-2(f)(6) and 1.704-2(j)(2). This
     Section 1(a) is intended to comply with the minimum gain chargeback requirement in Regulations Section
     1.704-2(f) and shall be interpreted consistently therewith.

              (b)       Member Minimum Gain Chargeback. Except as otherwise provided in Regulations
     Section 1.704-2(i)(4), notwithstanding any other provision of this Exhibit, if there is a net decrease in
     Member Nonrecourse Debt Minimum Gain attributable to a Member Nonrecourse Debt during any fiscal
     year, each Member who has a share of the Member Nonrecourse Debt Minimum Gain attributable to such
     Member Nonrecourse Debt, determined in accordance with Regulations Section 1.704-2(i)(5), shall be
     specially allocated items of Company income and gain for such fiscal year (and, if necessary, subsequent
     fiscal years) in an amount equal to such Member's share of the net decrease in Member Nonrecourse Debt,
     determined in accordance with Regulations Section 1.704-2(i)(4). Allocations pursuant to the previous
     sentence shall be made in proportion to the respective amounts required to be allocated to each Member
     pursuant thereto. The items to be so allocated shall be determined in accordance with Regulations Sections
     1.704-2(i)(4) and 1.704-2(j)(2). This Section 1(b) is intended to comply with the minimum gain chargeback
     requirement in Regulations Section 1.704-2(i)(4) and shall be interpreted consistently therewith.

              (c)       Qualified Income Offset. In the event any Member unexpectedly receives any
     adjustments, allocations, or distributions described in Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(d)(4), Section
     1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(d)(5), or Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(d)(6), items of Company income and gain shall be
     specially allocated to such Member in an amount and manner sufficient to eliminate, to the extent required
     by the Regulations, the deficit balance in the Capital Account of such Member as quickly as possible,
     provided, that an allocation pursuant to this Section 1(c) shall be made only if and to the extent that such
     Member would have an deficit balance in its Capital Account after all other allocations provided for in this
     Exhibit have been tentatively made as if this Section 1(c) were not in this Exhibit.

               (d)      Gross Income Allocation. In the event any Member has a deficit Capital Account at the
     end of any fiscal year that is in excess of the amount such Member is obligated to restore pursuant to the
     penultimate sentences of Regulations Sections 1.704-2(g)(1) and 1.704-2(i)(5), such Member shall be
     specially allocated items of Company income and gain in the amount of such excess as quickly as possible,
     provided, that an allocation pursuant to this Section 1(d) shall be made only if and to the extent that such
     Member would have a deficit Capital Account in excess of such amount after all other allocations provided
     for in this Exhibit have been made as if Section 1(c) and this Section 1(d) were not in this Agreement.

              (e)        Nonrecourse Deductions. Nonrecourse Deductions for any fiscal year shall be specially
     allocated to all of the Members in proportion to the Membership Interests of the Members.

              (f)     Member Nonrecourse Deductions. Any Member Nonrecourse Deductions for any fiscal
     year shall be specially allocated to the Member who bears the economic risk of loss with respect to the
     Member Nonrecourse Debt to which such Member Nonrecourse Deductions are attributable in accordance
     with Regulations Section 1.704-2(i)(1).



                                                     12

===== PAGE 262 =====
                   (g)      Section 754 Adjustments. To the extent an adjustment to the adjusted tax basis of any
         Company asset pursuant to Code Section 734(b) or Section 743(b) is required, pursuant to Regulations
         Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m)(2) or Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m)(4), to be taken into account in determining
         Capital Accounts as the result of a distribution to a Member in complete liquidation of such Member's
         interest in the Company, the amount of such adjustment to the Capital Accounts shall be treated as an item
         of gain (if the adjustment increases the basis of the asset) or as an item of loss (if the adjustment decreases
         such basis), and such gain or loss shall be specially allocated to the Members in accordance with their
         interests in the Company in the event Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m)(2) applies, or to the
         Member to whom such distribution was made in the event Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m)(4)
         applies.

         2.       Curative Allocations. The allocations set forth in Section 1 (the “Regulatory Allocations”) are
intended to comply with certain requirements of the Regulations. It is the intent of the Members that, to the extent
possible, all Regulatory Allocations shall be offset either with other Regulatory Allocations or with special
allocations of other items of Company income, gain, loss, or deduction pursuant to this Section 2. Therefore,
notwithstanding any other provision of this Exhibit (other than the Regulatory Allocations), the Manager shall make
such offsetting special allocations of Company income, gain, loss, or deduction in whatever manner it determines
appropriate so that, after such offsetting allocations are made, each Member's Capital Account balance is, to the
extent possible, equal to the Capital Account balance such Member would have had if the Regulatory Allocations
were not part of this Agreement and all Company items were allocated pursuant to Article 6(1) and Article 6(2). In
exercising their discretion under this Section 2, the Manager shall take into account future Regulatory Allocations
under Sections 1(a) and 1(b) that, although not yet made, are likely to offset other Regulatory Allocations previously
made under Sections 1(e) and 1(f).

         3.        Loss Limitation. Losses allocated pursuant to Article 6(1) hereof shall not exceed the maximum
amount of losses that can be allocated without causing any Member to have an deficit balance in its Capital Account
at the end of any fiscal year. In the event some but not all of the Members would have a deficit balance in its Capital
Account as a consequence of an allocation of losses pursuant to Article 6(1) hereof, the limitation set forth in this
Section 3 shall be applied on a Member by Member basis and losses not allocable to any Member as a result of such
limitation shall be allocated to the other Members in accordance with the positive balances in such Members’
Capital Accounts so as to allocate the maximum permissible losses to each Member under Regulations Section
1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(d).

         4.       Other Allocation Rules.

         (a)     For purposes of determining the profits, losses, or any other items allocable to any period, profits,
losses, and any such other items shall be determined on a daily, monthly, or other basis, as determined by the
Manager using any permissible method under Code Section 706 and the Regulations thereunder.

           (b)     Solely for purposes of determining a Member's proportionate share of the "excess nonrecourse
liabilities" of the Company within the meaning of Regulations Section 1.752-3(a)(3), the Members' interests in
Company profits are in proportion to the Membership Interests of such Members.

         (c)     In the event that the Company has taxable income that is characterized as ordinary income under
the recapture provisions of the Code, each Member's distributive share of taxable gain or loss from the sale of
Company assets shall (to the extent possible) include a proportionate share of this recapture income equal to such
Member's share of prior cumulative depreciation deductions with respect to the assets that gave rise to the recapture
income.

          (d)      It is the intention of the parties that the Capital Accounts of the Members immediately before the
liquidation of the Company shall be as nearly equal as possible to the amounts that they would receive in such
liquidation if Article 6(8) governed liquidating distributions. Therefore, in the fiscal year the Company is liquidated,
should there be any difference between the Capital Accounts of the Members and the amounts they would otherwise
be entitled to receive in liquidation if Article 6(8) governed, then profits and losses in such fiscal year shall be




                                                          13

===== PAGE 263 =====
specially allocated among the Members so that, to the maximum extent possible, their Capital Accounts shall equal
the amounts to which they would be entitled if Article 6(8) governed liquidating distributions.

        5.       Tax Allocations: Code Section 704(c). In accordance with Code Section 704(c) and the
Regulations thereunder, income, gain, loss, and deduction with respect to any property contributed to the capital of
the Company shall, solely for tax purposes, be allocated among the Members so as to take account of any variation
between the adjusted basis of such property to the Company for federal income tax purposes and its initial Gross
Asset Value using the "traditional method" pursuant to the Regulations under Code Section 704(c).

         In the event the Gross Asset Value of any Company asset is adjusted pursuant to subparagraph (ii) of the
definition of “Gross Asset Value” set forth below, subsequent allocations of income, gain, loss, and deduction with
respect to such asset shall take account of any variation between the adjusted basis of such asset for federal income
tax purposes and its Gross Asset Value in the same manner as under Code Section 704(c) and the Regulations
thereunder.

         Any elections or other decisions relating to such allocations shall be made by the Manager in any manner
that reasonably reflects the purpose and intention of this Agreement. Allocations pursuant to this Section 5 are solely
for purposes of federal, state, and local taxes and shall not affect, or in any way be taken into account in computing,
any Member's Capital Account or share of profits, losses, other items, or distributions pursuant to any provision of
this Agreement.

For purposes of this Exhibit A, the following definitions shall apply:

         (i)      “Company Minimum Gain” has the same meaning as is given to the term “partnership minimum
gain” in Sections 1.704-2(b)(2) and 1.704-2(d) of the Regulations.

         (ii)     “Company Nonrecourse Debt” has the same meaning as is given to the term “partner nonrecourse
debt” in Section 1.704-2(b)(4) of the Regulations.

         (iii)    “Company Nonrecourse Debt Minimum Gain” means an amount, with respect to each Member
Nonrecourse Debt, equal to the Company Minimum Gain that would result if such Member Nonrecourse Debt were
treated as a Nonrecourse Liability, determined in accordance with Section 1.704-2(i)(3) of the Regulations.

        (iv)    “Company Nonrecourse Deductions” has the same meaning as is given to the term “partner
nonrecourse deductions” in Sections 1.704-2(i)(1) and 1.704-2(i)(2) of the Regulations.

         (v)      “Gross Asset Value” means, with respect to any asset, the asset's adjusted basis for federal income
tax purposes, except as follows:

                  (i) The initial Gross Asset Value of any asset contributed by a Member to the Company shall be
         the gross fair market value of such asset, as determined by the contributing Member and the Manager.

                  (ii) The Gross Asset Values of all Company assets shall be adjusted to equal their respective gross
         fair market values, as determined by the Manager, as of the following times: (a) the acquisition of an
         additional interest in the Company by any new or existing Member in exchange for more than a de minimis
         Capital Contribution; (b) the distribution by the Company to any Member of more than a de minimis
         amount of property as consideration for an interest in the Company; and (c) the liquidation of the Company
         within the meaning of Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(ii)(g); provided, however, that adjustments
         pursuant to clauses (a) and (b) above shall be made only if the Manager reasonably determines that such
         adjustments are necessary or appropriate to reflect the relative economic interests of the Members in the
         Company.

                  (iii) The Gross Asset Value of any Company asset distributed to any Member shall be adjusted to
         equal the gross fair market value of such asset on the date of distribution as determined by the distributee
         and the Manager.




                                                          14

===== PAGE 264 =====
                   (iv)     The Gross Asset Values of Company assets shall be increased (or decreased) to reflect
         any adjustments to the adjusted basis of such assets pursuant to Code Section 734(b) or Section 743(b), but
         only to the extent that such adjustments are taken into account in determining Capital Accounts pursuant to
         Regulations Section 1.704-1(b)(2)(iv)(m) and subparagraph (vi) of the definition of “Profits” and “Losses”
         set forth in this definition of “Gross Asset Value”, or pursuant to Section 1(e) hereof; provided, however,
         that Gross Asset Values shall not be adjusted pursuant to this subparagraph (iv) to the extent that an
         adjustment pursuant to subparagraph (ii) of this definition is required in connection with a transaction that
         would otherwise result in an adjustment pursuant to this subparagraph (iv).

If the Gross Asset Value of an asset has been determined or adjusted pursuant to subparagraph (i), subparagraph (ii), or
subparagraph (iv) of this definition, such Gross Asset Value shall thereafter be adjusted by the depreciation taken into
account with respect to such asset for purposes of computing profits and losses.

         (vi)     “Nonrecourse Deductions” has the meaning set forth in Section 1.704-2(b)(1) of the Regulations.

         (vii)    “Nonrecourse Liability” has the meaning set forth in Section 1.704-2(b)(3) of the Regulations.

INDEPENDENT AUDITOR'S REPORT

To the Board of Directors
Scalabrini Villa, Inc. and Affiliate
North Kingstown, Rhode Island

We have audited the accompanying consolidated financial statements of Scalabrini Villa, Inc. and
Affiliate, which comptise the consolidated balance sheet as of December 31, 2017, and the related
consolidated statements of operations and changes in net assets, and cash flows for the year then
ended, and the related notes to the consolidated financial statements.

Management's Responsibility for the Financial Statements

Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of these consolidated financial
statements in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of Ametica;
this includes the design, implementation, and maintenance of internal control relevant to the
preparation and fait presentation of consolidated financial statements that are free from material
misstatement, whether due to fraud or ettor.

Auditor's Responsibility

Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these consolidated financial statements based on our
audit, We conducted out audit in accordance with auditing standatds genetally accepted in the United
States of America. Those standards require that we plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable
assutance about whether the consolidated financial statements ate free from material misstatement.

An audit involves performing procedures to obtain audit evidence about the amounts and disclosures in
the consolidated financial statements. The procedures selected depend on the auditor's judgment,
inchading the assessment of the tisks of material misstatement of the financial statements, whether due
to fraud ot ertor. In making those tisk assessments, the auditor considers internal control relevant to
the entity's preparation and fair presentation of the financial statements in order to design audit
procedures that are appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the putpose of expressing an opinion
on the effectiveness of the entity's internal control. Accordingly, we express no such opinion. An audit
also includes evaluating the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the teasonableness of
significant accounting estimates made by management, as well as evaluating the overall presentation of
the financial statements.

We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to ptovide a basis for
our audit opinion.

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION

Description of organization and principles of consolidation

The accompanying consolidated financial statements include the accounts and
transactions of two affiliated entities related through common management: Scalabrini
Villa, Inc. (the "Corporation") and The Pious Society of the Missionaries of St. Charles
Botomeo, Inc. d/b/a Scalabtini Villa (the "Society"). The two entities, which are
teferred to herein as the Home, ate governed by the Scalabrini Provincial (the
"Provincial"). The accompanying consolidated financial statements do not include the
accounts and transactions of the Provincial.

The Corpotation and the Society ate Rhode Island nonstock, nonprofit corporations
otganized (sponsored) by The Missionaries of St. Charles. The Society owns a 120-bed
nursing facility located in North Kingstown, Rhode Island. The Corporation opetates
the nursing facility, which is leased from the Society.

The Society is regulated by the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development
("HUD") as to operating methods undet Section 232 of the National Housing Act of
1959 (the "Act"). ‘The Section 232 Housing Program was fotmed under the Act and
provides HUD-insured mottgage notes to participating developments.

All significant intercompany accounts and transactions have been eliminated in the
accompanying consolidated financial statements.

SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES

Recently issued but not yet effective accounting pronouncements

Revenue recognition

In May 2014, Financial Accounting Standards Board ("ASB") issued ASU No. 2014-
09, Revenue from Contracts mith Customers ("ASU 2014-09"), which requires an entity to
recognize revenue to depict the transfer of promised goods ot setvices to customers in
an amount that reflects the considetation to which the entity expects to be entitled in
exchange fot those goods or services. This ASU will replace most existing revenue
recognition guidance in accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of
America ("U.S. GAAP"), including industry-specific guidance, when it becomes
effective. This new guidance is effective for years beginning after December 15, 2018.
The Home is evaluating the effect that ASU 2014-09 will have on its consolidated
financial statements and related disclosures.

Financial statement presentation

In August 2016, the FASB issued ASU No, 2016-14, Presentation of. Financial Statements of
Not-For Profit Entities (‘ASU 2016-14”), which amends the standards for the
presentation and accompanying disclosutes of financial statements of nonprofit
organizations. The ASU is effective for years beginning after December 15, 2017, and
will be applied on a retrospective basis. After the adoption of ASU 2016-14, the
Home’s financial statements will report two classes of net assets, distinguishing between
net assets with donor restrictions and those without donor testtictions. The financial
statements will also include an analysis of expenses by function and nature, and provide
enhanced disclosures about the Home’s liquidity and availability of its resources, and
donot restrictions and designations of its net assets.

Conttibutions, donot-imposed restrictions and net assets released from restrictions

Contribution revenue is recorded as received or unconditionally promised.
Contributions of cash, including collections of any future unconditional promises to
give, and other assets are reported as restricted support if they are received with donor
stipulations that limit the use of the donated assets. When a donor resttiction expires,
that is, when a stipulated time restriction ends or purpose restriction is accomplished,
temporarily restricted net assets ate reclassified to unrestricted net assets and repotted in
the consolidated statement of opetations and changes in net assets as “net assets
teleased from testtictions.” Donot-testticted contributions whose testrictions ate met in
the same teporting period are reported as untestticted suppott.

If a contribution is made in assets other than cash, the atnount of the contribution is
measured at the fair value of the asset at the date the conttibution or unconditional
ptomise to give is made by the donot.

Gifts of long-lived assets such as land, buildings, ot equipment ate teported as
untestricted support unless explicit donor stipulations specify how the donated assets
must be used. Gifts of long-lived assets with explicit restrictions that specify how the
assets ate to be used and gifts of cash ot other assets that must be used to acquite long-
lived assets ate reported as testricted support. Absent explicit donor stipulations about
how long those long-lived assets must be maintained, expirations of donor restrictions
ate reported when the donated or acquired long-lived assets are placed in service.

Contributed services

Contributed services received by the Home ate not recognized as support since such
services either do not require specialized skills or would not typically be purchased if not
provided by donation.

Accounts receivable
Patient accounts teceivable are stated at the amount the Home expects to collect.

Under Rhode Island General Law Title 40, Chapter 8, Section 6.1(b)(2) , Human Services,
Medical Assistance, Provider Care During Pendency of Application, Uncompensated Care While
Determination is Overdue, upon the Home’s request, the State of Rhode Island (the
“State”) is tequited to make payments to the Home fot charges related to a patient that
the Home has determined should be eligible for Medicaid and for whom a completed
application (as defined) for Medicaid benefits filed with the State has been pending for
90 days ot more (“advance payments”); such advance payrnents may not be recovered,
offset ot tecouped by the State upon a subsequent determination by the State that the
patient is not eligible for Medicaid. Consequently, advance payments received by the
Home prior to the date the State has made a determination related to the patient’s
eligibility for Medicaid are offset against the patient services receivable in the
accompanying consolidated balance sheet. Advance payments received by the Home
telated to Medicaid claims subsequently approved and paid by the State ate required to
be tepaid to the State through an upcoming reconciliation process and, consequently,
are teported within current liabilities, third-party advances in the accompanying
consolidated balance sheet. At December 31, 2017, advance payments offset against
patient services receivables totaled $630,000.

Fair value measurements

FASB Accounting Standards Codification ("ASC") 820, Fair Value Measurement,
establishes a framework for measuring fait value. That framework provides a fair value
hierarchy that prioritizes the inputs to valuation techniques used to measute fair value.
The hierarchy gives the highest priority to unadjusted quoted ptices in active markets
fot identical assets ot liabilities (Level 1) and the lowest priority to unobservable inputs
(Level 3). Categorization within the valuation hietarchy is based upon the lowest level of
input that is significant to the fait value measurement. Valuation techniques used need
to maximize the use of obsetvable inputs and minimize the use of unobservable inputs.
Under the standard, fair value is defined as the exit price, ot the amount that would be
received to sell an asset ot paid to transfer a liability in an orderly transaction between
market patticipants as of the measurement date.

The thtee levels of the fair value hierarchy are described as follows:

Level 1 inputs to the valuation methodology ate unadjusted quoted prices for
identical assets or liabilities in active markets that the Home has the ability to access.

Level 2 inputs to the valuation methodology include: quoted prices for similar assets
or liabilities in active markets; quoted prices for identical assets or liabilities in
inactive markets; inputs other than quoted prices that ate obsetvable for the asset ot
liability; and, inputs that are derived principally from or corroborated by obsetvable
market data by correlation or other means. If the asset ot liability has a specified
(contractual) term, the Level 2 input must be obsetvable for substantially the full
tetm of the asset ot liability.

Level 3 inputs to the valuation methodology ate unobservable and significant to the
fait value measutement.

months ot less to be cash equivalents, excluding money market funds included in the
investment portfolio, which the Home considers to be investments for financial
statement presentation putposes. The Home had cash equivalents of $1,208,000 at

neat term will matetially affect investment balances and the amounts teported in the

The Home evaluates its investments for other than temporary impaitment on an
ongoing basis. A decline in the fair value of an other than trading secutity below cost
that is deemed to be other than temporaty results in a new cost basis for the security
which is equivalent to its fair value. In evaluating other than temporaty impairment, the
Home considers its intent and ability to hold the investment until recovery and other
relevant factors. During the yeat ended December 31, 2017, thete wete no such

COMMITMENT

The Home is a participant in a pooled self-insurance workers! compensation trust (the
"Plan") with six other untelated not-for-profit nursing and assisted living facilities in the
State of Rhode Island (the "State"). The Plan provides for the payment of premiums
based on the workers’ compensation rates established by the State and each facility's
individual experience. Premiums ate intended to be sufficient to cover all operating
costs, including claims and tisk management costs, as well as payment for losses.

Each patticipating facility makes monthly premium payments to the Plan to pay cuttent
costs and losses and to establish a cumulative reserve for futute claims. The tesetve is
held in trust and invested by the Plan's trustee. The Plan maintains excess liability
insurance coverage with a commercial insurance catrier to protect the Plan's assets from
individual losses ia excess of $400,000. Each participating facility is jointly and severally
liable for the claims of the Plan.

MEDICAID ELIGIBILITY DETERMINATION DELAYS

Since eatly 2016, the State has been significantly delayed in tendering Medicaid eligibility
determinations with respect to patients that the Home has determined should be eligible
for Medicaid and for whom a completed application for Medicaid benefits has been
filed with the State. The Home ptovides setvices to these patients and recognizes
related tevenue during the petiod in which the State has not made a determination
telated to a completed application filed with the State (“Medicaid Pending
Application”). Amounts reported as patient services receivables telated to Medicaid
Pending Applications that have been pending for less than 90 days and those that have
been pending for 90 days or more for which the Home has not requested an advance
payment (see Note 2) may be subject to subsequent denial by the State at some futute
date. Management believes that the State will ultimately determine that patients for
whom a Medicaid Pending Application exists ate eligible for Medicaid and that any
potential loss related to Medicaid Pending Applications subsequently denied by the State
will not have a material adverse effect on the Home’s financial position.

INDEPENDENT AUDITOR'S REPORT

To the Boatd of Ditectors
Scalabrini Villa, Inc. and Affiliate
North Kingstown, Rhode Island

We have audited the accompanying consolidated financial statements of Scalabrini Villa, Inc. and
Affiliate, which comptise the consolidated balance sheet as of December 31, 2018, and the related
consolidated statements of operations, functional expenses and cash flows fot the year then ended, and
the related notes to the consolidated financial statements.

Management's Responsibility for the Financial Statements

Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of these consolidated financial
statements in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America;
this includes the design, implementation, and maintenance of internal control relevant to the
ptepatation and fair presentation of consolidated financial statements that ate free from material
misstatement, whether due to fraud or ertot.

Auditot's Responsibility

Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these consolidated financial statements based on out
audit, We conducted our audit in accordance with auditing standards generally accepted in the United
States of America. Those standards require that we plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable
assutance about whether the consolidated financial statements ate free from material misstatement.

An audit involves performing procedures to obtain audit evidence about the amounts and disclosutes in
the consolidated financial statements. The procedures selected depend on the auditor's judgment,
inclaiding the assessment of the risks of tmatetial misstatement of the financial statements, whether due
to fraud ot errot. In making those risk assessments, the auditor considers internal control relevant to
the entity's preparation and. fair presentation of the financial statements in otder to design audit
procedures that are appropriate in the citcumstances, but not for the purpose of exptessing an opinion
on the effectiveness of the entity's internal control. Accordingly, we exptess no such opinion. An audit
also includes evaluating the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of
significant accounting estimates made by management, as well as evaluating the overall presentation of
the financial statements.

We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and approptiate to ptovide a basis for
out audit opinion.

DESCRIPTION OF ORGANIZATION AND PRINCIPLES OF
CONSOLIDATION

The accompanying consolidated financial statements include the accounts and
transactions of two affiliated entities related through common management: Scalabrini
Villa, Inc. (the "Corporation") and The Pious Society of the Missionaries of St. Chatles
Boromeo, Inc. d/b/a Scalabrini Villa (the "Society"’). The two entities, which are
referred to herein as the Home, ate governed by the Scalabrini Provincial (the
"Ptovincial"). The accompanying consolidated financial statements do not include the
accoutits and transactions of the Provincial.

The Corporation and the Society ate Rhode Island nonstock, nonprofit corporations
otganized (sponsored) by The Missionaries of St. Charles. The Society owns a 120-bed
nutsing facility located in North Kingstown, Rhode Island. The Corporation operates
the nursing facility, which is leased from the Society.

The Society is regulated by the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development
("HUD") as to operating methods under Section 232 of the National Housing Act of
1959 (the "Act"). The Section 232 Housing Progtam was formed under the Act and
provides HUD-insured mortgage notes to patticipating developments.

All significant intercompany accounts and transactions have been eliminated in the
accompanying consolidated financial statements.

SUMMARY OF SIGNIFICANT ACCOUNTING POLICIES

Recently adopted accounting pronouncement

On August 18, 2016, the Financial Accounting Standards Board ("FASB") issued
Accounting Standards Update ("ASU") No. 2016-14, Not-for-Profit Entities (Topic 958) —
Presentation of Financial Statements of Not-for-Profit Entities. The update addresses the
complexity and undexstandability of net asset classification, deficiencies in information
about liquidity and availability of resources, and the lack of consistency in the type of
information ptovided about expenses and investment return. The Home has adjusted
the presentation of these consolidated financial statements accordingly.

Recently issued but not yet effective accounting pronouncements

Revenue recognition

In May 2014, the FASB issued ASU No. 2014-09, Revenue from Contracts with Customers
(Topic 606), as amended (commonly referred to as "ASC 606"), which requires an entity
to recognize revenue to depict the transfer of ptomised goods ot services to customers
in an amount that teflects the consideration which the entity expects to be entitled to
teceive in exchange for those goods or services. ASC 606 teplaces most existing
tevenue tecognition standards in accounting principles generally accepted in the United
States of America ("U.S. GAAP"), including industry-specific standards, when it
becomes effective. Fot annual reporting petiods, this standard is effective for the
Home on January 1, 2019. The standard permits the use of either the retrospective or
cumulative-effect transition method.
